Kaufmann and Koolen (2021)
as

\mathcal{C}_{G}(x)\mathrel{\triangleq}\min_{\lambda\in]1/2,1]}\frac{g_{G}(\lambda)+x}{\lambda}\text{ and }g_{G}(\lambda)\mathrel{\triangleq}2\lambda-2\lambda\log(4\lambda)+\log\zeta(2\lambda)-\frac{1}{2}\log(1-\lambda)\>.

(20)

Here,
\zeta
is the Riemann
\zeta
function and
\mathcal{C}_{G}(x)\approx x+\log(x)
.

We consider the concentration event
\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{\delta}=\bigcap_{a\neq a^{\star}}\bigcap_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{\delta}(a,n)
with
\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{\delta}(a,n)=

\left\{\frac{\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a},a}}{2\sigma^{2}}(\hat{\mu}_{k_{n,a},a}-\mu_{a})^{2}+\frac{\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a^{\star}},a^{\star}}}{2\sigma^{2}}(\hat{\mu}_{k_{n,a^{\star}},a^{\star}}-\mu_{a^{\star}})^{2}<c^{G}_{a,a^{\star}}(\tilde{N}_{k_{n}},\frac{\delta}{\zeta(s)^{2}k_{n,a}^{s}k_{n,a^{\star}}^{s}})\right\}\>.

(21)

For all
a\neq a^{\star}
and all
(k_{a},k_{a^{\star}})\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
, the estimators
(\hat{\mu}_{k_{c},c})_{c\in\{a,a^{\star}\}}
are based solely on the observations collected for arm
a
(resp. arm
a^{\star}
) between times
n\in\{T_{k_{a}-1}(a),\cdots,T_{k_{a}}(a)-1\}
(resp.
n\in\{T_{k_{a^{\star}}-1}(a^{\star}),\cdots,T_{k_{a^{\star}}}(a^{\star})-1\}
) with local counts
(\tilde{N}_{k_{c},c})_{c\in\{a,a^{\star}\}}
, i.e. dropping past observations.
Using a direct union bound, we obtain that
\mathbb{P}_{\nu}((\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{\delta})^{\complement})
is smaller than

\displaystyle\sum_{a\neq a^{\star}}\sum_{k_{a},k_{a^{\star}}\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\nu}}\left(\frac{\tilde{N}_{k_{a},a}}{2\sigma^{2}}(\hat{\mu}_{k_{a},a}-\mu_{a})^{2}+\frac{\tilde{N}_{k_{a^{\star}},a^{\star}}}{2\sigma^{2}}(\hat{\mu}_{k_{a^{\star}},a^{\star}}-\mu_{a^{\star}})^{2}\geq c^{G}_{a,a^{\star}}(\tilde{N}_{k_{n}},\frac{\delta}{\zeta(s)^{2}k_{a}^{s}k_{a^{\star}}^{s}})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta}{K-1}\frac{1}{\zeta(s)^{2}}\sum_{a\neq a^{\star}}\sum_{(k_{a},k_{a^{\star}})\in\mathbb{N}^{2}}\frac{1}{(k_{a}k_{a^{\star}})^{s}}=\delta\>.

where the last inequality uses Lemma
35
for all
a\neq a^{\star}
and all
(k_{a},k_{a^{\star}})\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
.
Therefore,

\mathbb{P}_{\nu}(\tau_{\delta}<+\infty,\>\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}\neq a^{\star})\leq\delta+\mathbb{P}_{\nu}(\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{\delta}\cap\{\tau_{\delta}<+\infty,\>\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}\neq a^{\star}\})\>.

Under
\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{\delta}\cap\{\tau_{\delta}<+\infty,\>\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}\neq a^{\star}\}
, we have
\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}=\argmax_{b\in[K]}\hat{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a},a}\neq a^{\star}
and

\displaystyle c^{G}_{\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}},a^{\star}}(\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta}}},\frac{\delta}{\zeta(s)^{2}k_{\tau_{\delta},a}^{s}k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}}^{s}})\leq\frac{(\hat{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}-\hat{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}})^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}(1/\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}+1/\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}})}

\displaystyle\quad=\inf_{y\geq x}\left\{\frac{\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}}{2\sigma^{2}}(\hat{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}-x)^{2}+\frac{\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}}}{2\sigma^{2}}(\hat{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}}-y)^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}}{2\sigma^{2}}(\hat{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}-\mu_{\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}})^{2}+\frac{\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}}}{2\sigma^{2}}(\hat{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}}-\mu_{a^{\star}})^{2}

\displaystyle\quad<c^{G}_{\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}},a^{\star}}(\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta}}},\frac{\delta}{\zeta(s)^{2}k_{\tau_{\delta},a}^{s}k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}}^{s}})\>.

This is a contradiction, hence
\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{\delta}\cap\{\tau_{\delta}<+\infty,\>\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}\neq a^{\star}\}=\emptyset
.
This concludes the proof.
∎

F.2
Private GLR with Non-private Transportation Cost: Proof of Lemma
21

The proof of Lemma
21
is similar as the one detailed in Appendix
F.1
, with the added difficulty of controlling the Laplace noise.
We consider the concentration event
\mathcal{E}_{\delta}=\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{\delta/2}\cap\mathcal{E}^{(2)}_{\delta/2}
where
\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{\delta}
as in Eq. (
21
) and
\mathcal{E}^{(2)}_{\delta}=\bigcap_{a\in[K]}\bigcap_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{E}^{(2)}_{\delta}(a,n)
with

\mathcal{E}^{(2)}_{\delta}(a,n)=\left\{\epsilon\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a},a}|Y_{k_{n,a},a}|<\log\left(\frac{K\zeta(s)k_{n,a}^{s}}{\delta}\right)\right\}\>.

(22)

Since
Y_{k_{n,a},a}\sim\text{Lap}\left((\epsilon\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a},a})^{-1}\right)
, we have that
\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a},a}|Y_{k_{n,a},a}|\sim\mathcal{E}(\epsilon)
for all
a\in[K]
and all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, where
\mathcal{E}(\cdot)
denotes the exponential distribution.
Using concentration results for exponential distribution, a direct union bound yields that
\mathbb{P}_{\nu}((\mathcal{E}^{(2)}_{\delta})^{\complement})\leq\delta
, hence

\mathbb{P}_{\nu}(\tau_{\delta}<+\infty,\>\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}\neq a^{\star})\leq\delta+\mathbb{P}_{\nu}(\mathcal{E}_{\delta}\cap\{\tau_{\delta}<+\infty,\>\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}\neq a^{\star}\})\>.

Under
\mathcal{E}_{\delta}\cap\{\tau_{\delta}<+\infty,\>\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}\neq a^{\star}\}
, we have
\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}=\argmax_{b\in[K]}\tilde{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a},a}\neq a^{\star}
and

\displaystyle c^{G,\epsilon}_{\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}},a^{\star}}(\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta}}},\delta)\leq\frac{\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}}{2\sigma^{2}}(\tilde{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}-\mu_{\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}})^{2}+\frac{\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}}}{2\sigma^{2}}(\tilde{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}}-\mu_{a^{\star}})^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}}{\sigma^{2}}(\hat{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}-\mu_{\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}})^{2}+\frac{\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}}}{\sigma^{2}}(\hat{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}}-\mu_{a^{\star}})^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}}{\sigma^{2}}Y_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}^{2}+\frac{\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}}}{\sigma^{2}}Y_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad<2c^{G}_{\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}},a^{\star}}(\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta}}},\frac{\delta}{2\zeta(s)^{2}k_{\tau_{\delta},a}^{s}k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}}^{s}})+\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}}\sum_{c\in\{a,a^{\star}\}}\frac{1}{\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},c},c}}\left(\log\frac{2K\zeta(s)k_{\tau_{\delta},c}^{s}}{\delta}\right)^{2}\>.

where we used that
\tilde{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a},a}=\tilde{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a},a}+Y_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a},a}
and
(x-y)^{2}\leq 2x^{2}+2y^{2}
.
This is a contradiction, hence
\mathcal{E}_{\delta}\cap\{\tau_{\delta}<+\infty,\>\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}\neq a^{\star}\}=\emptyset
.
To conclude the proof,
we take as a specific parameter
s=2
, hence
\zeta(2)=\pi^{2}/6
.

F.3
Private GLR with Adapted Transportation Cost: Proof of Lemma
23

The proof of Lemma
23
is similar as the one detailed in Appendix
F.2
.
The main difference lies in the considered transportation costs, namely
W^{G,\epsilon}_{a,b}
instead of
W^{G}_{a,b}
.

Lemma 36

(Lemma 28 in
Jourdan et al. 2023
)
.

Let
\delta\in(0,1)
.
For all
x\geq 1
, let
\overline{W}_{-1}(x)=-W_{-1}(-e^{-x})
(see Lemma
40
), where
W_{-1}
is the negative branch of the Lambert
W
function.
Let
c(x,\delta)=\frac{1}{2}\overline{W}_{-1}\left(2\log\left(K/\delta\right)+4\log(4+\log x)+1/2\right)
.
Consider
\sigma
-sub-Gaussian bandits with means
\mu\in\mathbb{R}^{K}
.
Then,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\exists n\in\mathbb{N},\>\exists a\in[K],\>\frac{N_{n,a}}{2\sigma^{2}}(\mu_{n,a}-\mu_{a})^{2}>c(N_{n,a},\delta)\right)\leq\delta\>.

Recall that
h(\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a},a},\delta)=c(\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a},a},\frac{\delta}{3\zeta(s)k_{n,a}^{s}})
.
We use the concentration event
\mathcal{E}_{\delta}=\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{\delta/3}\cap\mathcal{E}^{(2)}_{\delta/3}\cap\mathcal{E}^{(3)}_{\delta/3}
where
\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{\delta}
as in Eq. (
21
),
\mathcal{E}^{(2)}_{\delta}
as in Eq. (
22
) and
\mathcal{E}^{(3)}_{\delta}=\bigcap_{a\in[K]}\bigcap_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{E}^{(3)}_{\delta}(a,n)
with

\mathcal{E}^{(3)}_{\delta}(a,n)=\left\{\frac{\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a},a}}{2\sigma^{2}}(\hat{\mu}_{k_{n,a},a}-\mu_{a})^{2}<h(\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a},a},3\delta)\right\}\>.

Using Lemma
36
, a direct union bound yields that
\mathbb{P}_{\nu}((\mathcal{E}^{(3)}_{\delta})^{\complement})\leq\delta
, hence

\mathbb{P}_{\nu}(\tau_{\delta}<+\infty,\>\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}\neq a^{\star})\leq\delta+\mathbb{P}_{\nu}(\mathcal{E}_{\delta}\cap\{\tau_{\delta}<+\infty,\>\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}\neq a^{\star}\})\>.

Under
\mathcal{E}_{\delta}\cap\{\tau_{\delta}<+\infty,\>\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}\neq a^{\star}\}
, we have
\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}=\argmax_{b\in[K]}\tilde{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a},a}\neq a^{\star}
.

Case 1.

Under
\mathcal{E}_{\delta}\cap\{\tau_{\delta}<+\infty,\>\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}\neq a^{\star},\>(\hat{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}-\hat{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}})_{+}<\epsilon/2\}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}c^{G,\epsilon}_{\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}},a^{\star}}(\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta}}},\delta)+\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\epsilon\sigma}\sum_{c\in\{\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}},a^{\star}\}}\sqrt{\frac{h(\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},c}},\delta)}{\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},c}}}}\log\left(\frac{3K\zeta(s)k_{\tau_{\delta},c}^{s}}{\delta}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}}{2\sigma^{2}}(\tilde{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}-\mu_{\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}})^{2}+\frac{\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}}}{2\sigma^{2}}(\tilde{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}}-\mu_{a^{\star}})^{2}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}}{2\sigma^{2}}(\hat{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}-\mu_{\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}})^{2}+\frac{\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}}}{2\sigma^{2}}(\hat{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}}-\mu_{a^{\star}})^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}}{2\sigma^{2}}Y_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}^{2}+\frac{\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}}}{2\sigma^{2}}Y_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}}{\sigma^{2}}Y_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}(\hat{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}-\mu_{\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}})+\frac{\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}}}{\sigma^{2}}Y_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}}(\hat{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}}-\mu_{a^{\star}})

\displaystyle\quad<\frac{1}{2}c^{G,\epsilon}_{\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}},a^{\star}}(\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta}}},\delta)+\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\epsilon\sigma}\sum_{c\in\{\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}},a^{\star}\}}\sqrt{\frac{h(\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},c}},\delta)}{\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},c}}}}\log\left(\frac{3K\zeta(s)k_{\tau_{\delta},c}^{s}}{\delta}\right)\>.

This is a contradiction, hence
\mathcal{E}_{\delta}\cap\{\tau_{\delta}<+\infty,\>\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}\neq a^{\star}\>(\hat{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}-\hat{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}})_{+}<\epsilon/2\}=\emptyset
.

Case 2.

Under
\mathcal{E}_{\delta}\cap\{\tau_{\delta}<+\infty,\>\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}\neq a^{\star},\>(\hat{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}-\hat{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}})_{+}\geq\epsilon/2\}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\log\left(\frac{3K\zeta(s)\max_{c\in\{\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}},a^{\star}\}}k_{\tau_{\delta},c}}{\delta}\right)+\frac{\epsilon}{2\sqrt{2}\sigma}\sum_{c\in\{\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}},a^{\star}\}}\sqrt{\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},c}}h(\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},c}},\delta)}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{\epsilon(\tilde{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}})}{4\sigma^{2}(1/\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}+1/\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}})}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{\epsilon}{4\sigma^{2}}\min\{\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}})\}(\tilde{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}})

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{\epsilon}{4\sigma^{2}}\inf_{y\geq x}\left\{\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}|\tilde{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}-x|+\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}}|\tilde{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}}-y|\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{\epsilon}{4\sigma^{2}}\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}|\tilde{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}-\mu_{\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}|+\frac{\epsilon}{4\sigma^{2}}\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}}|\tilde{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}}-\mu_{a^{\star}}|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{\epsilon}{4\sigma^{2}}\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}|\hat{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}-\mu_{\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}|+\frac{\epsilon}{4\sigma^{2}}\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}}|\hat{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}}-\mu_{a^{\star}}|

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\epsilon}{4\sigma^{2}}\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}|Y_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}|+\frac{\epsilon}{4\sigma^{2}}\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}}|Y_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}}|

\displaystyle\quad<\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\log\left(\frac{3K\zeta(s)\max_{c\in\{\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}},a^{\star}\}}k_{\tau_{\delta},c}}{\delta}\right)+\frac{\epsilon}{2\sqrt{2}\sigma}\sum_{c\in\{\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}},a^{\star}\}}\sqrt{\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},c}}h(\tilde{N}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},c}},\delta)}\>.

This is a contradiction, hence
\mathcal{E}_{\delta}\cap\{\tau_{\delta}<+\infty,\>\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}\neq a^{\star}\>(\hat{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}},\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}}-\hat{\mu}_{k_{\tau_{\delta},a^{\star}},a^{\star}})_{+}\geq\epsilon/2\}=\emptyset
.

Summary.
Putting both cases together
and take as a specific parameter
s=2
yields the result.

Appendix G
Expected Sample Complexity of
\mathsf{AdaP\text{-}TT}
and
\mathsf{AdaP\text{-}TT}^{\star}

Let
\beta\in(0,1)
,
\epsilon\in\mathbb{R}^{\star}_{+},
and
\bm{\nu}
be a bandit instance consisting of
\sigma
-sub-Gaussian distributions with distinct means
\bm{\mu}\in\mathbb{R}^{K}
, i.e.
\min_{a\neq b}|\mu_{a}-\mu_{b}|>0
.
For conciseness, we denote
\Delta_{a}\mathrel{\triangleq}\mu_{a^{\star}}-\mu_{a}
,
\Delta_{\min}\mathrel{\triangleq}\min_{a\neq a^{\star}}\Delta_{a}
,
and
\Delta_{\max}\mathrel{\triangleq}\max_{a\neq a^{\star}}\Delta_{a}
.
For Gaussian, we define the unique
\beta
-optimal allocations
\omega^{\star}_{\mathrm{KL},\beta}(\bm{\nu})=\{(\omega^{\star}_{\beta,a})_{a\in[K]}\}
and
\omega^{\star}_{\mathrm{KL},\beta}(\bm{\nu}_{G,\epsilon})=\{(\omega^{\star}_{\epsilon,\beta,a})_{a\in[K]}\}
as

\omega^{\star}_{\mathrm{KL},\beta}(\bm{\nu})\mathrel{\triangleq}\argmax_{\omega\in\Sigma_{K},\omega_{a^{\star}}=\beta}\min_{a\neq a^{\star}}\frac{\Delta_{a}^{2}}{1/\beta+1/\omega_{a}}\>\text{ , }\>\omega^{\star}_{\mathrm{KL},\beta}(\bm{\nu}_{G,\epsilon})\mathrel{\triangleq}\argmax_{\omega\in\Sigma_{K},\omega_{a^{\star}}=\beta}\min_{a\neq a^{\star}}\frac{\Delta_{a}\min\{\epsilon/2,\Delta_{a}\}}{1/\beta+1/\omega_{a}}\>.

At equilibrium, we have equality of the transportation costs (see
Jourdan and Degenne (2024)
for example), namely

\forall a\neq a^{\star},\quad\frac{\Delta_{a}^{2}}{1/\beta+1/\omega^{\star}_{\beta,a}}=2\sigma^{2}T^{\star}_{\mathrm{KL},\beta}(\bm{\nu})^{-1}\quad\text{,}\quad\frac{\Delta_{a}\min\{\epsilon/2,\Delta_{a}\}}{1/\beta+1/\omega^{\star}_{\epsilon,\beta,a}}=2\sigma^{2}T^{\star}_{\mathrm{KL},\beta}(\bm{\nu}_{G,\epsilon})^{-1}\>.

(23)

Our proof follows the unified sample complexity analysis of Top Two algorithms from
Jourdan et al. (2022)
.

Let
\gamma>0
.
Let
\omega\in\Sigma_{K}
be any allocation over arms such that
\min_{a}\omega_{a}>0
.
We denote by
T_{\bm{\mu},\gamma}(\omega)
the
convergence time
towards
\omega
, which is a random variable quantifying the number of samples required for the global empirical allocations
N_{n}/(n-1)
to be
\gamma
-close to
\omega
for any subsequent time, namely

T_{\bm{\mu},\gamma}(\omega)\mathrel{\triangleq}\inf\left\{T\geq 1\mid\forall n\geq T,\>\left\|\frac{N_{n}}{n-1}-\omega\right\|_{\infty}\leq\gamma\right\}\>.

(24)

As the
\mathsf{AdaP\text{-}TT}
and
\mathsf{AdaP\text{-}TT}^{\star}
algorithms share the same leader, all results solely on the leader applies to both of them.
As the
\mathsf{AdaP\text{-}TT}
and
\mathsf{AdaP\text{-}TT}^{\star}
algorithms consider a TC challenger with different transportation costs, all results involving the challenger should be slightly modified.
Except if specified otherwise, all the results presented in the following hold for both algorithms.

The rest of Appendix
G
is organised as follows.
After recalling some technical results (Appendix
G.1
), we prove sufficient exploration (Appendix
G.2
)
Second, we prove that convergence towards the
\beta
-optimal allocation (Appendix
G.3
) in finite time.
Third, we explicit the cost of doubling and forgetting (Appendix
G.4
).
Finally, we conclude the proof of Theorems
22
and
24
(Appendix
G.5
).

G.1
Technical Results

Before delving into the proofs, we first recall some useful technical results.

Doubling trick.

Due to the doubling, the growth of the counts is exponential (Lemma
37
).

Lemma 37
.

For all
(a,k)\in[K]\times\mathbb{N}
s.t.
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[T_{k}(a)]<+\infty
,
N_{T_{k}(a),a}=2^{k-1}
and
\tilde{N}_{k,a}=2^{k-2}
.

Proof.

Let
a\in[K]
.
After initialisation, we have
k=1
,
T_{1}(a)=K+1
and
N_{T_{1}(a),a}=1
.
Using the definition of the phase switch, it is direct to see that
N_{T_{2}(a),a}=2
and
\tilde{N}_{2,a}=1
when
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[T_{2}(a)]<+\infty
.

Now, we proceed by recurrence.
Suppose that
N_{T_{k}(a),a}=2^{k-1}
and
\tilde{N}_{k,a}=2^{k-2}
when
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[T_{k}(a)]<+\infty
.
If
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[T_{k+1}(a)]<+\infty
, then it means that the phase
k
ends for arm
a
almost surely.
Since we sample only one arm at each round, at the beginning of phase
k+1
for arm
a
, we have
N_{T_{k+1}(a),a}=2N_{T_{k}(a),a}=2^{k}
by using the definition of the phase switch.
Then, we have directly that
\tilde{N}_{k+1,a}=N_{T_{k+1}(a),a}-N_{T_{k}(a),a}=2^{k}-2^{k-1}=2^{k-1}
.
∎

Tracking.

We denote by
N^{a}_{n,b}\mathrel{\triangleq}\sum_{t\in[n-1]}\mathds{1}\left(B_{t}=a,\>a_{t}=C_{t}=b\right)
the number of times the arm
b
was pulled while the arm
a
was the leader, and by
L_{n,a}\mathrel{\triangleq}\sum_{t\in[n-1]}\mathds{1}\left(B_{t}=a\right)
the number of times arm
a
was the leader.

Lemma 38

(Lemma 2.2 in
Jourdan and Degenne 2024
)
.

For all
n>K
and all
a\in[K]
, we have
-1/2\leq N_{n,a}^{a}-\beta L_{n,a}\leq 1
.

Concentration results.

In order to control the randomness of
(\tilde{\mu}_{k_{a},a})_{a\in[K]}
, we use a standard concentration result on the empirical mean of sub-Gaussian random variables and on sub-exponential observations (Lemma
39
).
Since Bernoulli distributions are
1/2
-sub-Gaussian and the absolute value of a Laplace is an exponential distribution, Lemma
39
applies to our setting.

Lemma 39
.

There exists a sub-Gaussian random variable
W_{\mu}
such that, almost surely,

\displaystyle\forall a\in[K],\>\forall k_{a}\in\mathbb{N},\quad|\hat{\mu}_{k_{a},a}-\mu_{a}|\leq W_{\mu}\sqrt{\frac{\log(e+\tilde{N}_{k_{a},a})}{\tilde{N}_{k_{a},a}}}\>.

There exists a sub-exponential random variable
W_{\epsilon}
such that, almost surely,

\displaystyle\forall a\in[K],\>\forall k_{a}\in\mathbb{N},\quad|Y_{k_{a},a}|\leq W_{\epsilon}\frac{\log(e+k_{a})}{\tilde{N}_{k_{a},a}}\>.

In particular, any random variable which is polynomial in
(W_{\epsilon},W_{\mu})
has a finite expectation.

Proof.

The first part is a known result, e.g. Appendix E.2 in
Jourdan et al. (2022)
.
Let

W_{\epsilon}\mathrel{\triangleq}\sup_{a\in[K]}\sup_{k_{a}\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\tilde{N}_{k_{a},a}|Y_{k_{a},a}|}{\log(e+k_{a})}\>.

By definition, we have that, almost surely,

\displaystyle\forall a\in[K],\>\forall k_{a}\in\mathbb{N},\quad|Y_{k_{a},a}|\leq W_{\epsilon}\frac{\log(e+k_{a})}{\tilde{N}_{k_{a},a}}\>.

Since
\tilde{N}_{k,i}|Y_{k,i}|\sim\mathcal{E}(\epsilon)
, Lemma 72 in
Jourdan et al. (2022)
yields that
W_{\epsilon}
is a sub-exponential random variable.
Since
W_{\mu}
is sub-Gaussian and
W_{\epsilon}
is a sub-exponential, any random variable which is polynomial in
(W_{\epsilon},W_{\mu})
has a finite expectation.
∎

Inversion results.

Lemma
40
gathers properties on the function
\overline{W}_{-1}
, which is used in the literature to obtain concentration results.

Lemma 40

(
Jourdan et al. 2023
)
.

Let
\overline{W}_{-1}(x)\mathrel{\triangleq}-W_{-1}(-e^{-x})
for all
x\geq 1
, where
W_{-1}
is the negative branch of the Lambert
W
function.
The function
\overline{W}_{-1}
is increasing on
(1,+\infty)
and strictly concave on
(1,+\infty)
.
In particular,
\overline{W}_{-1}^{\prime}(x)=\left(1-\frac{1}{\overline{W}_{-1}(x)}\right)^{-1}
for all
x>1
.
Then, for all
y\geq 1
and
x\geq 1
,

\overline{W}_{-1}(y)\leq x\quad\iff\quad y\leq x-\log(x)\>.

Moreover, for all
x>1
,

x+\log(x)\leq\overline{W}_{-1}(x)\leq x+\log(x)+\min\left\{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{\sqrt{x}}\right\}\>.

Lemma
41
is an inversion result to upper bound a time, which is implicitly defined.
It is a direct consequence of Lemma
40
.

Lemma 41
.

Let
\overline{W}_{-1}
defined in Lemma
40
.
Let
A>0
,
B>0
such that
B/A+\log A>1
and

\displaystyle C(A,B)=\sup\left\{x\mid\>x<A\log x+B\right\}\>.

Then,
C(A,B)<h_{1}(A,B)
with
h_{1}(z,y)=z\overline{W}_{-1}\left(y/z+\log z\right)
.

Proof.

Since
B/A+\log A>1
, we have
C(A,B)\geq A
, hence

C(A,B)=\sup\left\{x\mid\>x<A\log(x)+B\right\}=\sup\left\{x\geq A\mid\>x<A\log(x)+B\right\}\>.

Using Lemma
40
yields that

\displaystyle x\geq A\log x+B\>\iff\>\frac{x}{A}-\log\left(\frac{x}{A}\right)\geq\frac{B}{A}+\log A\>\iff\>x\geq A\overline{W}_{-1}\left(\frac{B}{A}+\log A\right)\>.

∎

G.2
Sufficient Exploration

The first step of in the generic analysis of Top Two algorithms
Jourdan et al. (2022)
consists in showing sufficient exploration.
The main idea is that, if there are still undersampled arms, either the leader or the challenger will be among them.
Therefore, after a long enough time, no arm can still be undersampled.
We emphasise that there are multiple ways to select the leader/challenger pair in order to ensure sufficient exploration.
Therefore, other choices of leader/challenger pair would yield similar results.

Given an arbitrary phase
p\in\mathbb{N}
, we define the sampled enough set, i.e. the arms having reached phase
p
, and the arm with highest mean in this set (when not empty) as

S_{n}^{p}=\{a\in[K]\mid N_{n,a}\geq 2^{p-1}\}\quad\text{and}\quad a_{n}^{\star}=\argmax_{a\in S_{n}^{p}}\mu_{a}\>.

(25)

Since
\min_{a\neq b}|\mu_{a}-\mu_{b}|>0
,
a_{n}^{\star}
is unique.
Let
p\in\mathbb{N}
such that
(p-1)/4\in\mathbb{N}
.
We define the highly and the mildly under-sampled sets as

U_{n}^{p}\mathrel{\triangleq}\{a\in[K]\mid N_{n,a}<2^{(p-1)/2}\}\quad\text{and}\quad V_{n}^{p}\mathrel{\triangleq}\{a\in[K]\mid N_{n,a}<2^{3(p-1)/4}\}\>.

(26)

Those arms have not reached phase
(p-1)/2
and phase
3(p-1)/4
, respectively.

Lemma
42
shows that, when the leader is sampled enough, it is the arm with highest true mean among the sampled enough arms.

Lemma 42
.

Let
S_{n}^{p}
and
a_{n}^{\star}
as in (
25
).
There exists
p_{0}
with
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[\exp(\alpha p_{0})]<+\infty
for all
\alpha>0
such that if
p\geq p_{0}
, for all
n
such that
S_{n}^{p}\neq\emptyset
,
B_{n}\in S_{n}^{p}
implies that
B_{n}=a_{n}^{\star}=\argmax_{a\in S_{n}^{p}}\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a},a}
.

Proof.

Let
p_{0}
to be specified later.
Let
p\geq p_{0}
.
Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that
S_{n}^{p}\neq\emptyset
, where
S_{n}^{p}
and
a_{n}^{\star}
as in Equation (
25
).
Let
(k_{n,a})_{a\in[K]}
be the phases indices for all arms.
Since
N_{n,a}\geq 2^{p-1}
for all
a\in S_{n}^{p}
, we have
k_{n,a}\geq p
and
\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a},a}\geq 2^{p-2}
by using Lemma
37
. Using Lemma
39
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a_{n}^{\star}},a_{n}^{\star}}

\displaystyle\geq\mu_{a_{n}^{\star}}-W_{\mu}\sqrt{\frac{\log(e+2^{p-2})}{2^{p-2}}}-W_{\epsilon}\frac{\log(e+p)}{2^{p-2}}\>,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a},a}

\displaystyle\leq\mu_{a}+W_{\mu}\sqrt{\frac{\log(e+2^{p-2})}{2^{p-2}}}+W_{\epsilon}\frac{\log(e+p)}{2^{p-2}}\>,\quad\forall a\in S_{n}^{p}\setminus\{a_{n}^{\star}\}.

Here, we use that
x\to\log(e+x)/x
is decreasing.

Let
\overline{\Delta}_{\min}=\min_{a\neq b}|\mu_{a}-\mu_{b}|
. By assumption on the considered instances, we know that
\overline{\Delta}_{\min}>0
.
Let
p_{1}=\lceil\log_{2}(X_{1}-e)\rceil+2
and
p_{2}=\lceil\log_{2}((X_{2}-e-2)\log 2+1)\rceil+2
with

\displaystyle X_{1}

\displaystyle=\sup\left\{x>1\mid\>x\leq 64\overline{\Delta}_{\min}^{-2}W_{\mu}^{2}\log x+e\right\}\leq h_{1}(64\overline{\Delta}_{\min}^{-2}W_{\mu}^{2},\>e)\>,

\displaystyle X_{2}

\displaystyle=\sup\left\{x>1\mid\>x\leq\frac{8}{\log 2}\overline{\Delta}_{\min}^{-1}W_{\epsilon}\log x+e+2-1/\log 2\right\}\leq h_{1}(8\overline{\Delta}_{\min}^{-1}W_{\epsilon}/\log 2,\>4)\>,

where we used Lemma
41
, and
h_{1}
defined therein.
Then, for all
p\in\mathbb{N}
such that
p\geq\max\{p_{1},p_{2}\}+1
and all
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that
S_{n}^{p}\neq\emptyset
, we have
\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a_{n}^{\star}},a_{n}^{\star}}\geq\mu_{a_{n}^{\star}}-\overline{\Delta}_{\min}/4
and
\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a},a}\leq\mu_{a}+\overline{\Delta}_{\min}/4
for all
a\in S_{n}^{p}\setminus\{a_{n}^{\star}\}
, hence
a_{n}^{\star}=\argmax_{a\in[K]}\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a},a}
.

We have, for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
,

\exp(\alpha p_{1})\leq e^{3\alpha}(X_{1}-e)^{\alpha/\log 2}\quad\text{hence}\quad\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[\exp(\alpha p_{1})]<+\infty\>,

where we used Lemma
39
and
h_{1}(x,e)\sim_{x\to+\infty}x\log x
to obtain that
\exp(\alpha p_{1})
is at most polynomial in
W_{\mu}
.
Likewise, we obtain that
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[\exp(\alpha p_{2})]<+\infty
for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
.

Let us define the UCB indices by
I_{k_{n,a},a}=\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a},a}+\sqrt{k_{n,a}/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a},a}}+k_{n,a}/(\epsilon\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a},a})
.
Using the above, we have

\displaystyle I_{k_{n,a_{n}^{\star}},a_{n}^{\star}}\geq\mu_{a_{n}^{\star}}-W_{\mu}\sqrt{\frac{\log(e+2^{p-2})}{2^{p-2}}}-W_{\epsilon}\frac{\log(e+p)}{2^{p-2}}\>,

\displaystyle\forall a\in S_{n}^{p}\setminus\{a_{n}^{\star}\},\quad

\displaystyle I_{k_{n,a},a}\leq\mu_{a}+W_{\mu}\sqrt{\frac{\log(e+2^{p-2})}{2^{p-2}}}+W_{\epsilon}\frac{\log(e+p)}{2^{p-2}}+\sqrt{\frac{p}{2^{p-2}}}+\frac{p}{\epsilon 2^{p-2}}\>,

where we used Lemma
37
and the fact that
x\to\log(e+x)/x
and
x\to x2^{2-x}
are decreasing function for
x\geq 2
.
Let
p_{3}=\lceil\log_{2}X_{3}\rceil+2
and
p_{4}=\lceil\log_{2}X_{4}\rceil+2
with

\displaystyle X_{3}

\displaystyle=\sup\left\{x>1\mid\>x\leq 64\overline{\Delta}_{\min}^{-2}(\log_{2}x+2)\right\}\leq h_{1}(64\overline{\Delta}_{\min}^{-2}/\log 2,\>128\overline{\Delta}_{\min}^{-2})\>,

\displaystyle X_{4}

\displaystyle=\sup\left\{x>1\mid\>x\leq 8\epsilon^{-1}\overline{\Delta}_{\min}^{-1}(\log_{2}x+2)\right\}\leq h_{1}(8\epsilon^{-1}\overline{\Delta}_{\min}^{-1}/\log 2,\>16\overline{\Delta}_{\min}^{-1}\epsilon^{-1})\>,

where we used Lemma
41
, and
h_{1}
defined therein.
We highlight that
(p_{3},p_{4})
are deterministic values, hence their expectation is finite.
Then, for all
p\in\mathbb{N}
such that
p\geq p_{0}=\max\{p_{1},p_{2},p_{3},p_{4}\}+1
and all
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that
S_{n}^{p}\neq\emptyset
, we have
I_{k_{n,a_{n}^{\star}},a_{n}^{\star}}\geq\mu_{a_{n}^{\star}}-\overline{\Delta}_{\min}/4
and
I_{k_{n,a},a}\leq\mu_{a}+\overline{\Delta}_{\min}/2
for all
a\in S_{n}^{p}\setminus\{a_{n}^{\star}\}
, hence
a_{n}^{\star}=B_{n}
since we have
B_{n}=\argmax_{a\in[K]}I_{k_{n,a},a}
.

Since we have
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[\exp(\alpha p_{0})]<+\infty
for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
, this concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma
43
shows that the transportation costs between the sampled enough arms with largest true means and the other sampled enough arms are increasing fast enough.

Lemma 43
.

Let
S_{n}^{p}
and
a_{n}^{\star}
as in Eq. (
25
).
There exists
p_{1}
with
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[\exp(\alpha p_{1})]<+\infty
for all
\alpha>0
such that if
p\geq p_{1}
, for all
n
such that
S_{n}^{p}\neq\emptyset
, for all
b\in S_{n}^{p}\setminus\{a_{n}^{\star}\}
, we have

\displaystyle\text{[{$\mathsf{AdaP\text{-}TT}$}{}]}\quad\frac{\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a_{n}^{\star}},a_{n}^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b},b}}{\sqrt{1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a_{n}^{\star}},a_{n}^{\star}}+1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,b},b}}}\geq 2^{p/2}C_{\mu}\>,

\displaystyle\text{[{$\mathsf{AdaP\text{-}TT}^{\star}$}{}]}\quad\frac{(\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a_{n}^{\star}},a_{n}^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b},b})\min\{\epsilon/2,\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a_{n}^{\star}},a_{n}^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b},b}\}}{1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a_{n}^{\star}},a_{n}^{\star}}+1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,b},b}}\geq 2^{p}C_{\mu}\>,

where
C_{\mu}>0
is a problem dependent constant.

Proof.

Let
p_{1}
to be specified later.
Let
p\geq p_{1}
.
Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that
S_{n}^{p}\neq\emptyset
, where
S_{n}^{p}
and
a_{n}^{\star}
as in Equation (
25
).
Let
(k_{n,a})_{a\in[K]}
be the phases indices for all arms.
Since
N_{n,a}\geq 2^{p-1}
for all
a\in S_{n}^{p}
, we have
k_{n,a}\geq p
and
\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a},a}\geq 2^{p-2}
by using Lemma
37
.
Let
\overline{\Delta}_{\min}=\min_{a\neq b}|\mu_{a}-\mu_{b}|
, which satisfies
\overline{\Delta}_{\min}>0
by assumption on the instance considered.

Using Lemma
39
, for all
b\in S_{n}^{p}\setminus\{a_{n}^{\star}\}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a_{n}^{\star}},a_{n}^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b},b}\geq\overline{\Delta}_{\min}-W_{\mu}\sqrt{\frac{\log(e+2^{p-2})}{2^{p-4}}}-W_{\epsilon}\frac{\log(e+p)}{2^{p-3}}\>.

Let
p_{3}=\lceil\log_{2}((X_{3}-e)/4)\rceil+4
and
p_{2}=\lceil\log_{2}((X_{2}-e-3)\log 2+1)\rceil+3
with

\displaystyle X_{3}

\displaystyle=\sup\left\{x>1\mid\>x\leq 64\overline{\Delta}_{\min}^{-2}W_{\mu}^{2}\log x+e\right\}\leq h_{1}(64\overline{\Delta}_{\min}^{-2}W_{\mu}^{2},\>e)\>,

\displaystyle X_{2}

\displaystyle=\sup\left\{x>1\mid\>x\leq 4\overline{\Delta}_{\min}^{-1}W_{\epsilon}\log x+e+3-1/\log 2\right\}\leq h_{1}(4\overline{\Delta}_{\min}^{-1}W_{\epsilon},\>5)\>,

where we used Lemma
41
, and
h_{1}
defined therein.
Then, for all
p\in\mathbb{N}
such that
p\geq p_{1}=\max\{p_{3},p_{2}\}+1
and all
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that
S_{n}^{p}\neq\emptyset
, we have, for all
b\in S_{n}^{p}\setminus\{a_{n}^{\star}\}
,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a_{n}^{\star}},a_{n}^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b},b}\geq\overline{\Delta}_{\min}/2\>.

As in the proof of Lemma
42
, we obtain that
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[\exp(\alpha p_{1})]<+\infty
for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
.

Then, for all
b\in S_{n}^{p}\setminus\{a_{n}^{\star}\}
, we have

\frac{\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a_{n}^{\star}},a_{n}^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b},b}}{\sqrt{1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a_{n}^{\star}},a_{n}^{\star}}+1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,b},b}}}\geq 2^{p/2}\frac{\overline{\Delta}_{\min}}{2^{5/2}}\>,

where we used that
\min\{\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a_{n}^{\star}},\tilde{N}_{k_{n,b},b}}\}\geq 2^{p-2}
.
Setting
C_{\mu}=\overline{\Delta}_{\min}/2^{5/2}
yields the first result.

The second result is obtained similarly by taking
C_{\mu}=\frac{\overline{\Delta}_{\min}}{16}\min\{\epsilon/2,\frac{\overline{\Delta}_{\min}}{2}\}

∎

Lemma
44
shows that the transportation costs between sampled enough arms and undersampled arms are not increasing too fast.

Lemma 44
.

Let
S_{n}^{p}
be as in Eq. (
25
).
For all
p\geq 1
and all
n
such that
S_{n}^{p}\neq\emptyset
, for all
a\in S_{n}^{p}
and
b\notin S_{n}^{p}
,

\displaystyle\text{[{$\mathsf{AdaP\text{-}TT}$}{}]}\quad\frac{\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a},a}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b},b}}{\sqrt{1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a},a}+1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,b},b}}}\leq 2^{p/2}D_{\mu}+2W_{\mu}\sqrt{\log(e+2^{p-2})}+2W_{\epsilon}\log(e+p)\>,\>

\displaystyle\text{[{$\mathsf{AdaP\text{-}TT}^{\star}$}{}]}\quad\frac{(\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a},a}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b},b})\min\{\epsilon/2,\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a},a}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b},b}\}}{1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a},a}+1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,b},b}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\leq 2^{p}D_{\mu}+8W_{\mu}^{2}\log(e+2^{p-2})+8W_{\epsilon}^{2}\log(e+p)^{2}\>,

where
D_{\mu}>0
is a problem dependent constant and
(W_{\mu},W_{\epsilon})
are the random variables defined in Lemma
39
.

Proof.

Let
p\geq 1
.
Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that
S_{n}^{p}\neq\emptyset
, where
S_{n}^{p}
as in Equation (
25
).
Let
(k_{n,a})_{a\in[K]}
be the phases indices for all arms.
Since
N_{n,a}\geq 2^{p-1}
for all
a\in S_{n}^{p}
, we have
k_{n,a}\geq p
and
\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a},a}\geq 2^{p-2}
by using Lemma
37
.
Likewise,
N_{n,a}<2^{p-1}
for all
a\notin S_{n}^{p}
, we have
k_{n,a}<p
and
\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a},a}<2^{p-2}
.
Let
\overline{\Delta}_{\max}=\min_{a\neq b}|\mu_{a}-\mu_{b}|
, which satisfies
\overline{\Delta}_{\max}>0
by assumption on the instance considered.
Using Lemma
39
, for all
a\in S_{n}^{p}
and
b\notin S_{n}^{p}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a},a}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b},b}}{\sqrt{1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a},a}+1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,b},b}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\tilde{N}_{k_{n,b},b}}(\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a},a}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b},b})

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\tilde{N}_{k_{n,b},b}}(\mu_{a}-\mu_{b})+2W_{\mu}\sqrt{\log(e+\tilde{N}_{k_{n,b},b})}+2W_{\epsilon}\frac{\log(e+k_{n,b})}{\sqrt{\tilde{N}_{k_{n,b},b}}}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{(p-2)/2}\overline{\Delta}_{\max}+2W_{\mu}\sqrt{\log(e+2^{p-2})}+2W_{\epsilon}\log(e+p)

where we used that
\tilde{N}_{k_{n,b},b}\geq 1
,
k_{n,b}<p
,
\tilde{N}_{k_{n,b},b}<2^{p-2}\leq\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a},a}
and
x\to\log(e+x)/x
is decreasing.
Taking
D_{\mu}=\overline{\Delta}_{\max}/2
yields the first result.

The proof of the second result follows along the same line by noting that this transportation cost is lower than the other:

\displaystyle\frac{(\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a},a}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b},b})\min\{\epsilon/2,\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a},a}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b},b}\}}{1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a},a}+1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,b},b}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\tilde{N}_{k_{n,b},b}(\hat{\mu}_{k_{n,a},a}-\hat{\mu}_{k_{n,b},b})^{2}+2\tilde{N}_{k_{n,b},b}(Y_{k_{n,a},a}-Y_{k_{n,b},b})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p}\overline{\Delta}_{\max}^{2}+8W_{\mu}^{2}\log(e+2^{p-2})+8W_{\epsilon}^{2}\log(e+p)^{2}\>.

Taking
D_{\mu}=\overline{\Delta}_{\max}^{2}
yields the result.
∎

Lemma
45
shows that the challenger is mildly undersampled if the leader is not mildly undersampled.

Lemma 45
.

Let
V_{n}^{p}
be as in Equation (
26
).
There exists
p_{2}
with
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[\exp(\alpha p_{2})]<+\infty
for all
\alpha>0
such that if
p\geq p_{2}
, for all
n
such that
U_{n}^{p}\neq\emptyset
,
B_{n}\notin V_{n}^{p}
implies
C_{n}\in V_{n}^{p}
.

Proof.

Let
p_{2}
to be specified later.
Let
p\geq p_{2}
.
Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that
U_{n}^{p}\neq\emptyset
and
V_{n}^{p}\neq[K]
, where
U_{n}^{p}\subseteq V_{n}^{p}
are defined in Equation (
26
).
In the following, we suppose that
B_{n}\notin V_{n}^{p}
.

Let
(k_{n,a})_{a\in[K]}
be the phases indices for all arms.
Let
p_{0}
as in Lemma
42
.
Let
b_{n}^{\star}=\argmax_{b\notin V_{n}^{p}}\mu_{b}
.
Then, for all
p\geq 4p_{0}/3-1/3
and all
n
such that
B_{n}\notin V_{n}^{p}
, Lemma
42
yields that
B_{n}=b_{n}^{\star}=\argmax_{a\notin V_{n}^{p}}\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a},a}
.

Let
p_{1}
and
C_{\mu}
as in Lemma
43
, and
D_{\mu}
as in Lemma
44
.
Then, for all
p\geq\frac{4}{3}\max\{p_{0},p_{1}\}-1/3
and all
n
such that
B_{n}\notin V_{n}^{p}
, we have
B_{n}=b_{n}^{\star}
and

\displaystyle\forall b\notin V_{n}^{p},\quad\frac{\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b_{n}^{\star}},b_{n}^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b},b}}{\sqrt{1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,b_{n}^{\star}},b_{n}^{\star}}+1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,b},b}}}

\displaystyle\geq 2^{(3p+1)/8}C_{\mu}\>,

\displaystyle\forall b\in U_{n}^{p},\quad\frac{\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b_{n}^{\star}},b_{n}^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b},b}}{\sqrt{1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,b_{n}^{\star}},b_{n}^{\star}}+1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,b},b}}}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{(p+1)/4}D_{\mu}+2W_{\mu}\sqrt{\log(e+2^{(p+1)/2-2})}

\displaystyle\quad+2W_{\epsilon}\log(e+(p+1)/2)\>,

where we used the first results of Lemmas
43
and
44
.
Let
p_{3}=16\lceil\log_{2}(2D_{\mu}/C_{\mu})\rceil+1
, then we have
2^{(p-1)/16}>\frac{D_{\mu}}{C_{\mu}}
for all
p\geq p_{3}
.
Let
p_{4}=\frac{16}{9}\lceil\log_{2}X_{4}\rceil+25
and
p_{5}=\frac{32}{9}\lceil\log_{2}X_{5}\rceil+7
where

\displaystyle X_{4}

\displaystyle=\sup\left\{x>1\mid x\leq\frac{W_{\mu}^{2}}{C_{\mu}^{2}}\log(e+x^{8/9}2^{25/18-3/4})\right\}\>,

\displaystyle X_{5}

\displaystyle=\sup\left\{x>1\mid x\leq\frac{2W_{\epsilon}}{C_{\mu}}\log(e+4+32\log_{2}(x)/18)\right\}\>.

As in the proof of Lemma
42
, using Lemma
39
yields that
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[\exp(\alpha p_{4})]<+\infty
and
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[\exp(\alpha p_{5})]<+\infty
for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
.
Let
p_{2}=\max\{p_{3},p_{4},p_{5},4\max\{p_{0},p_{1}\}/3-1/3\}+1
.
Then, we have shown that for all
p\geq p_{2}
, for all
n
such that
B_{n}\notin V_{n}^{p}
, we have
B_{n}=b_{n}^{\star}
and

\min_{b\notin V_{n}^{p}}\frac{\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b_{n}^{\star}},b_{n}^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b},b}}{\sqrt{1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,b_{n}^{\star}},b_{n}^{\star}}+1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,b},b}}}>\max_{b\in U_{n}^{p}}\frac{\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b_{n}^{\star}},b_{n}^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b},b}}{\sqrt{1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,b_{n}^{\star}},b_{n}^{\star}}+1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,b},b}}}\>,

Therefore, by definition of the TC challenger
C_{n}=\argmin_{b\neq b_{n}^{\star}}\frac{\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b_{n}^{\star}},b_{n}^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b},b}}{\sqrt{1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,b_{n}^{\star}},b_{n}^{\star}}+1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,b},b}}}
, we obtain that
C_{n}\in V_{n}^{p}
.
Otherwise, there would be a contradiction given that we assumed that
U_{n}^{p}\neq\emptyset
.
Given all the condition exhibited above, it is direct to see that
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[\exp(\alpha p_{2})]<+\infty
for all
\alpha>0
.
This concludes the proof for the
\mathsf{AdaP\text{-}TT}
algorithm.

For the
\mathsf{AdaP\text{-}TT}^{\star}
algorithm, the proof is done similarly based on the second results of Lemmas
43
and
44
.
As above, we can construct
\tilde{p}_{3}
, with
\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\exp(\alpha\tilde{p}_{3})]<+\infty
for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
, such that for all
p\geq\tilde{p}_{3}
, we have

2^{(3p+1)/4}C_{\mu}>2^{(p+1)/2}D_{\mu}+8W_{\mu}^{2}\log(e+2^{(p+1)/2-2})+8W_{\epsilon}^{2}\log(e+(p+1)/2)^{2}\>.

Let
\tilde{p}_{2}=\max\{\tilde{p}_{3},4\max\{p_{0},p_{1}\}/3-1/3\}+1
.
Then, we have shown that for all
p\geq\tilde{p}_{2}
, for all
n
such that
B_{n}\notin V_{n}^{p}
, we have
B_{n}=b_{n}^{\star}
and

\displaystyle\min_{b\notin V_{n}^{p}}\frac{(\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b_{n}^{\star}},b_{n}^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b},b})\min\{\epsilon/2,\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b_{n}^{\star}},b_{n}^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b},b}\}}{1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,b_{n}^{\star}},b_{n}^{\star}}+1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,b},b}}

\displaystyle>\max_{b\in U_{n}^{p}}\frac{(\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b_{n}^{\star}},b_{n}^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b},b})\min\{\epsilon/2,\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b_{n}^{\star}},b_{n}^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b},b}\}}{1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,b_{n}^{\star}},b_{n}^{\star}}+1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,b},b}}\>.

Then, we conclude similarly by using the definition of the TC challenger.
∎

Lemma
46
shows that all the arms are sufficient explored for large enough
n
.

Lemma 46
.

There exists
N_{0}
with
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[N_{0}]<+\infty
such that for all
n\geq N_{0}
and all
a\in[K]
,

N_{n,a}\geq\sqrt{n/K}\quad\text{and}\quad k_{n,a}\geq\frac{\log(n/K)}{2\log 2}+1\>.

Proof.

Let
p_{0}
and
p_{2}
as in Lemmas
42
and
45
.
Combining Lemmas
42
and
45
yields that, for all
p\geq p_{3}=\max\{p_{2},4p_{0}/3-1/3\}
and all
n
such that
U_{n}^{p}\neq\emptyset
, we have
B_{n}\in V_{n}^{p}
or
C_{n}\in V_{n}^{p}
.
We have
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[2^{p_{2}}]<+\infty
.
We have
2^{p-1}\geq K2^{3(p-1)/4}
for all
p\geq p_{4}=4\lceil\log_{2}K\rceil+1
.
Let
p\geq\max\{p_{3},p_{4}\}
.

Suppose towards contradiction that
U_{K2^{p-1}}^{p}
is not empty.
Then, for any
1\leq t\leq K2^{p-1}
,
U_{t}^{p}
and
V_{t}^{p}
are non empty as well.
Using the pigeonhole principle, there exists some
a\in[K]
such that
N_{2^{p-1},a}\geq 2^{3(p-1)/4}
.
Thus, we have
\left|V_{2^{p-1}}^{p}\right|\leq K-1
.
Our goal is to show that
\left|V_{2^{p}}^{p}\right|\leq K-2
.
A sufficient condition is that one arm in
V_{2^{p-1}}^{p}
is pulled at least
2^{3(p-1)/4}
times between
2^{p-1}
and
2^{p}-1
.

Case 1.
Suppose there exists
a\in V_{2^{p-1}}^{p}
such that
L_{2^{p},a}-L_{2^{p-1},a}\geq\frac{2^{3(p-1)/4}}{\beta}+3/(2\beta)
.
Using Lemma
38
, we obtain

N^{a}_{2^{p},a}-N^{a}_{2^{p-1},a}\geq\beta(L_{2^{p},a}-L_{2^{p-1},a})-3/2\geq 2^{3(p-1)/4}\>,

hence
a
is sampled
2^{3(p-1)/4}
times between
2^{p-1}
and
2^{p}-1
.

Case 2.
Suppose that for all
a\in V_{2^{p-1}}^{p}
, we have
L_{2^{p},a}-L_{2^{p-1},a}<2^{3(p-1)/4}/\beta+3/(2\beta)
.
Then,

\sum_{a\notin V_{2^{p-1}}^{p}}(L_{2^{p},a}-L_{2^{p-1},a})\geq 2^{p-1}-K\left(2^{3(p-1)/4}/\beta+3/(2\beta)\right)

Using Lemma
38
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{a\notin V_{2^{p-1}}^{p}}(N^{a}_{2^{p},a}-N^{a}_{2^{p-1},a})-\beta\sum_{a\notin V_{2^{p-1}}^{p}}(L_{2^{p},a}-L_{2^{p-1},a})\right|\leq 3(K-1)/2\>.

Combining all the above, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{a\notin V_{2^{p-1}}^{p}}(L_{2^{p},a}-L_{2^{p-1},a})-\sum_{a\notin V_{2^{p-1}}^{p}}(N^{a}_{2^{p},a}-N^{a}_{2^{p-1},a})

\displaystyle\geq(1-\beta)\sum_{a\notin V_{2^{p-1}}^{p}}(L_{2^{p},a}-L_{2^{p-1},a})-3(K-1)/2

\displaystyle\geq(1-\beta)\left(2^{p-1}-K\left(2^{3(p-1)/4}/\beta+3/(2\beta)\right)\right)-3(K-1)/2\geq K2^{3(p-1)/4}\>,

where the last inequality is obtained for
p\geq p_{5}
with

p_{5}=\sup\left\{p\in\mathbb{N}\mid(1-\beta)\left(2^{p-1}-K\left(2^{3(p-1)/4}/\beta+\frac{3}{2\beta}\right)\right)-\frac{3}{2}(K-1)<K2^{3(p-1)/4}\right\}\>.

The LHS summation is exactly the number of times where an arm
a\notin V_{2^{p-1}}^{p}
was leader but wasn’t sampled, hence

\sum_{t=2^{p-1}}^{2^{p}-1}\mathds{1}\left(B_{t}\notin V_{2^{p-1}}^{p},\>a_{t}=C_{t}\right)\geq K2^{3(p-1)/4}

For any
2^{p-1}\leq t\leq 2^{p}-1
,
U_{t}^{p}
is non-empty, hence we have
B_{t}\notin V_{2^{p-1}}^{p}
(hence
B_{t}\notin V_{t}^{p}
) implies
C_{t}\in V_{t}^{p}\subseteq V_{2^{p-1}}^{p}
.
Therefore, we have shown that

\sum_{t=2^{p-1}}^{2^{p}-1}\mathds{1}\left(a_{t}\in V_{2^{p-1}}^{p}\right)\geq\sum_{t=2^{p-1}}^{2^{p}-1}\mathds{1}\left(B_{t}\notin V_{2^{p-1}}^{p},\>a_{t}=C_{t}\right)\geq K2^{3(p-1)/4}\>.

Therefore, there is at least one arm in
V_{2^{p-1}}^{p}
that is sampled
2^{3(p-1)/4}
times between
2^{p-1}
and
2^{p}-1
.

In summary, we have shown
\left|V_{2^{p}}^{p}\right|\leq K-2
for all
p\geq p_{6}=\max\{p_{3},p_{4},p_{5}\}
.
By induction, for any
1\leq k\leq K
, we have
\left|V_{k2^{p-1}}^{p}\right|\leq K-k
, and finally
U_{K2^{p-1}}^{p}=\emptyset
for all
p\geq p_{6}
.
Defining
N_{0}=K2^{p_{6}-1}
, we have
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[N_{0}]<+\infty
by using Lemmas
42
and
45
for
p_{3}=\max\{p_{2},4p_{0}/3-1/3\}
and
p_{4}
and
p_{5}
are deterministic.
For all
n\geq N_{0}
, we let
2^{p-1}=\frac{n}{K}
.
Then, by applying the above, we have
U_{K2^{p-1}}^{p}=U^{\log_{2}(n/K)+1}_{n}
is empty, which shows that
N_{n,a}\geq\sqrt{n/K}
for all
a\in[K]
.
Using Lemma
37
, we obtain that
k_{n,a}\geq\frac{\log(n/K)}{2\log 2}+1
for all
a\in[K]
.
This concludes the proof.
∎

G.3
Convergence Towards
\beta
-optimal Allocation

The second step of in the generic analysis of Top Two algorithms
Jourdan et al. (2022)
is to show the convergence of the empirical proportions towards the
\beta
-optimal allocation.
First, we show that the leader coincides with the best arm. Hence, the tracking procedure will ensure that the empirical proportion of time we sample it is exactly
\beta
.
Second, we show that a sub-optimal arm whose empirical proportion overshoots its
\beta
-optimal allocation will not be sampled next as challenger.
Therefore, this “overshoots implies not sampled” mechanism will ensure the convergence towards the
\beta
-optimal allocation.
We emphasise that there are multiple ways to select the leader/challenger pair in order to ensure convergence towards the
\beta
-optimal allocation.
Therefore, other choices of leader/challenger pair would yield similar results.
Note that our results heavily rely on having obtained sufficient exploration first.

Convergence for the best arm.

Lemma
47
exhibits a random phase which ensures that the leader and the candidate answer are equal to the best arm for large enough
n
.

Lemma 47
.

Let
N_{0}
be as in Lemma
46
.
There exists
N_{1}\geq N_{0}
with
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[N_{1}]<+\infty
such that, for all
n\geq N_{1}
, we have
\hat{a}_{n}=B_{n}=a^{\star}
.

Proof.

Let
k\geq 1
.
Suppose that
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[\max_{a\in[K]}T_{k}(a)]<+\infty
.
Then, Lemma
37
yields that
N_{T_{k}(a),a}=2^{k-1}
and
\tilde{N}_{k,a}=2^{k-2}
.
Using Lemma
39
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mu}_{k,a^{\star}}\geq\mu_{a^{\star}}-W_{\mu}\sqrt{\frac{\log(e+2^{k-2})}{2^{k-2}}}-W_{\epsilon}\frac{\log(e+k)}{2^{k-2}}\>,

\displaystyle\forall a\neq a^{\star},\quad

\displaystyle\tilde{\mu}_{k,a}\leq\mu_{a}+W_{\mu}\sqrt{\frac{\log(e+2^{k-2})}{2^{k-2}}}+W_{\epsilon}\frac{\log(e+k)}{2^{k-2}}\>.

Let
p_{1}=\lceil\log_{2}(X_{1}-e)\rceil+2
and
p_{2}=\lceil\log_{2}(X_{2}-e-1)\rceil+2
with

\displaystyle X_{1}

\displaystyle=\sup\left\{x>1\mid\>x\leq 64\Delta_{\min}^{-2}W_{\mu}^{2}\log x+e\right\}\leq h_{1}(64\Delta_{\min}^{-2}W_{\mu}^{2},\>e)\>,

\displaystyle X_{2}

\displaystyle=\sup\left\{x>1\mid\>x\leq 8\Delta_{\min}^{-1}W_{\epsilon}\log x+e+1\right\}\leq h_{1}(8\Delta_{\min}^{-1}W_{\epsilon},\>e+1)\>,

\displaystyle X_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sup\left\{x>1\mid\>x\leq 8\Delta_{\min}^{-1}W_{\epsilon}\log(e+2+\log x)\right\}\>,

where we used Lemma
41
, and
h_{1}
defined therein.
Then, for all
k\in\mathbb{N}^{K}
such that
\min_{a\in[K]}k_{a}>p_{0}=\max\{p_{1},p_{2}\}
such that
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[\max_{a\in[K]}T_{k_{a}}(a)]<+\infty
, we have
\tilde{\mu}_{k,a^{\star}}\geq\mu_{a^{\star}}-\Delta_{\min}/4
and
\tilde{\mu}_{k,a}\leq\mu_{a}+\Delta_{\min}/4
for all
a\neq a^{\star}
, hence
a^{\star}=\argmax_{a\in[K]}\tilde{\mu}_{k,a}
.
We have, for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
,

\exp(\alpha p_{1})\leq e^{3\alpha}(X_{1}-e)^{\alpha/\log 2}\quad\text{hence}\quad\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[\exp(\alpha p_{1})]<+\infty\>,

where we used Lemma
39
and
h_{1}(x,e)\sim_{x\to+\infty}x\log x
to obtain that
\exp(\alpha p_{1})
is at most polynomial in
W_{\mu}
.
Likewise, we obtain that
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[\exp(\alpha p_{2})]<+\infty
for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
.
Therefore, we have
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[\exp(\alpha p_{0})]<+\infty
for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
.

Let us define the UCB indices by
I_{k,a}=\tilde{\mu}_{k,a}+\sqrt{k/\tilde{N}_{k,a}}+k/(\epsilon\tilde{N}_{k,a})
.
Using the above, we have

\displaystyle I_{k,a^{\star}}\geq\mu_{a^{\star}}-W_{\mu}\sqrt{\frac{\log(e+2^{k-2})}{2^{k-2}}}-W_{\epsilon}\frac{\log(e+k)}{2^{k-2}}+\frac{k}{\epsilon 2^{k-2}}\>,

\displaystyle\forall a\neq a^{\star},\quad

\displaystyle I_{k,a}\leq\mu_{a}+W_{\mu}\sqrt{\frac{\log(e+2^{k-2})}{2^{k-2}}}+W_{\epsilon}\frac{\log(e+k)}{2^{k-2}}+\frac{k}{\epsilon 2^{k-2}}\>.

Therefore, we have
a^{\star}=\argmax_{a\in[K]}I_{k,a}
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}^{K}
such that
\min_{a}k_{a}>\max\{p_{1},p_{2}\}
such that
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[\max_{a\in[K]}T_{k_{a}}(a)]<+\infty
.

Let
N_{0}
as in Lemma
46
.
Using Lemma
46
, we obtain that, for all
n\geq N_{0}
and all
a\in[K]
,
k_{n,a}\geq\log_{2}(n/K)/2+1
.
Therefore, we obtain
\min_{a\in[K]}k_{n,a}>\max\{p_{1},p_{2}\}
is implied by
n\geq N_{1}=\max\{K4^{\max\{p_{1},p_{2}\}},N_{0}\}
.
Using the above, we conclude that
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[N_{1}]<+\infty
and
\hat{a}_{n}=B_{n}=a^{\star}
for all
n\geq N_{1}
.
∎

Lemma
48
shows that that the pulling proportion of the best arm converges towards
\beta
, provided the phase defined in Lemma
47
is reached in finite time for all arms.

Lemma 48
.

Let
\gamma>0
, and
N_{1}
be as in Lemma
47
.
There exists a deterministic constant
C_{0}\geq 1
such that, for all
n\geq C_{0}N_{1}
,

\left|\frac{N_{n,a^{\star}}}{n-1}-\beta\right|\leq\gamma\>.

Proof.

Let
\gamma>0
.
Let
N_{1}
as in Lemma
47
.
Let
M\geq N_{1}
.
Using Lemma
47
, we obtain
B_{n}=a^{\star}
for all
n\geq M
.
Therefore, we obtain
L_{n,a^{\star}}\geq n-M
and
\sum_{a\neq a^{\star}}N^{a}_{n,a^{\star}}\leq M
for all
n\geq M
.
Using Lemma
38
yields that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{N_{n,a^{\star}}}{n-1}-\beta\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|N^{a^{\star}}_{n,a^{\star}}-\beta L_{n,a^{\star}}|}{n-1}+\beta\left|\frac{L_{n,a^{\star}}}{n-1}-1\right|+\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{a\neq a^{\star}}N^{a}_{n,a^{\star}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2(n-1)}+\beta\frac{2(M-1)}{n-1}\leq\gamma\>,

where the last inequality is obtained by taking
n\geq\max\{M,(1/2+2\beta(M-1))/\gamma+1\}
.
∎

Convergence for the sub-optimal arms.

Lemma
49
exhibits a random phase which ensures that if a sub-optimal arm overshoots its
\beta
-optimal allocation then it cannot be selected as challenger for large enough
n
.

Lemma 49
.

Let
\gamma>0
. Let
N_{1}
and
C_{0}
be as in Lemma
47
and
48
.
There exists
N_{2}\geq C_{0}N_{1}
with
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[N_{2}]<+\infty
such that, for all
n\geq N_{2}
,

\displaystyle\quad\exists a\neq a^{\star},\quad\frac{N_{n,a}}{n-1}\geq\gamma+\begin{cases}\omega^{\star}_{\beta,a}&\text{[{$\mathsf{AdaP\text{-}TT}$}{}]}\\
\omega^{\star}_{\epsilon,\beta,a}&\text{[{$\mathsf{AdaP\text{-}TT}^{\star}$}{}]}\end{cases}\quad\implies\quad C_{n}\neq a\>,

Proof.

Let
\gamma>0
and
\tilde{\gamma}>0
.
Let
N_{1}
as in Lemma
47
and
C_{0}
as in Lemma
48
for
\tilde{\gamma}
.
Let
n\geq C_{0}N_{1}
.

Let
a\neq a^{\star}
such that
\frac{N_{n,a}}{n-1}\geq\omega^{\star}_{\beta,a}+\gamma
.
Suppose towards contradiction that
\frac{N_{n,b}}{n-1}>\omega^{\star}_{\beta,a}
for all
b\notin\{a^{\star},a\}
.
Then, for all
n\geq C_{0}N_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle 1-\beta+\tilde{\gamma}\geq 1-\frac{N_{n,a^{\star}}}{n-1}=\sum_{b\neq a^{\star}}\frac{N_{n,b}}{n-1}>\gamma+\sum_{b\neq a^{\star}}\omega^{\star}_{\beta,b}=1-\beta+\gamma\>,

which yields a contradiction for
\tilde{\gamma}\leq\gamma
.
Therefore, for all
n\geq C_{0}N_{1}
, we have

\exists a\neq a^{\star},\quad\frac{N_{n,a}}{n-1}\geq\omega^{\star}_{\beta,a}+\gamma\quad\implies\quad\exists b\notin\{a^{\star},a\},\quad\frac{N_{n,b}}{n-1}\leq\omega^{\star}_{\beta,b}\>.

Then, we have

\sqrt{\frac{1+N_{n,a^{\star}}/N_{n,b}}{1+N_{n,a^{\star}}/N_{n,a}}}\geq\sqrt{\frac{1+(\beta-\tilde{\gamma})/\omega^{\star}_{\beta,b}}{1+(\beta+\tilde{\gamma})/(\omega^{\star}_{\beta,a}+\gamma)}}\>.

In the following, we use Lemma
39
and similar manipulations as in the proof of Lemma
47
.
Therefore, we obtain that, for all
c\neq a^{\star}
,

\displaystyle\left|\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a^{\star}},a^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,c},c}-\Delta_{c}\right|

\displaystyle\leq W_{\mu}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log(e+2^{k_{n,a^{\star}}-2})}{2^{k_{n,a^{\star}}-2}}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(e+2^{k_{n,c}-2})}{2^{k_{n,c}-2}}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+W_{\epsilon}\left(\frac{\log(e+k_{n,a^{\star}})}{2^{k_{n,a^{\star}}-2}}+\frac{\log(e+k_{n,c})}{2^{k_{n,c}-2}}\right)\>.

Let
p_{3}=\lceil\log_{2}(X_{1}-e)\rceil+2
and
p_{2}=\lceil\log_{2}(X_{2}-e-1)\rceil+2
with

\displaystyle X_{3}

\displaystyle=\sup\left\{x>1\mid\>x\leq 16\eta^{-2}W_{\mu}^{2}\log x+e\right\}\leq h_{1}(16\eta^{-2}W_{\mu}^{2},\>e)\>,

\displaystyle X_{2}

\displaystyle=\sup\left\{x>1\mid\>x\leq 4\eta^{-1}W_{\epsilon}\log x+e+1\right\}\leq h_{1}(4\eta^{-1}W_{\epsilon},\>e+1)\>,

\displaystyle X_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sup\left\{x>1\mid\>x\leq 4\eta^{-1}W_{\epsilon}\log(e+2+\log x)\right\}\>,

where we used Lemma
41
, and
h_{1}
defined therein.
We have, for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
,

\exp(\alpha p_{3})\leq e^{3\alpha}(X_{3}-e)^{\alpha/\log 2}\quad\text{hence}\quad\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[\exp(\alpha p_{3})]<+\infty\>,

where we used Lemma
39
and
h_{1}(x,e)\sim_{x\to+\infty}x\log x
to obtain that
\exp(\alpha p_{3})
is at most polynomial in
W_{\mu}
.
Likewise, we obtain that
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[\exp(\alpha p_{2})]<+\infty
for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
.

Using Lemma
46
(with
C_{0}N_{1}\geq N_{1}\geq N_{0}
), we obtain that, for all
n\geq C_{0}N_{1}
and all
a\in[K]
,
k_{n,a}\geq\log_{2}(n/K)/2+1
.
Therefore, we obtain
\min_{a\in[K]}k_{n,a}>\max\{p_{2},p_{3}\}
is implied by
n\geq N_{2}=\max\{K4^{\max\{p_{3},p_{2}\}},C_{0}N_{1}\}
.
Using the above, we conclude that
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[N_{2}]<+\infty
and
\max_{c\neq a^{\star}}|\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a^{\star}},a^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,c},c}-\Delta_{c}|\leq\eta
for all
n\geq N_{2}
.

Then, for all
n\geq N_{2}
, we have
B_{n}=a^{\star}
and

\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a^{\star}},a^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a},a}}{\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a^{\star}},a^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b},b}}\sqrt{\frac{1+N_{n,a^{\star}}/N_{n,b}}{1+N_{n,a^{\star}}/N_{n,a}}}\geq\frac{\Delta_{a}-\eta}{\Delta_{b}+\eta}\sqrt{\frac{1+(\beta-\tilde{\gamma})/\omega^{\star}_{\beta,b}}{1+(\beta+\tilde{\gamma})/(\omega^{\star}_{\beta,a}+\gamma)}}>1\>,

where the last inequality is obtained by taking
\eta
and
\tilde{\gamma}
sufficiently small and by using (
23
)

\frac{\Delta_{a}}{\Delta_{b}}\sqrt{\frac{1+\beta/\omega^{\star}_{\beta,b}}{1+\beta/\omega^{\star}_{\beta,a}}}=1\>.

Therefore, we have shown that
B_{n}=a^{\star}
and

\frac{\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a^{\star}},a^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a},a}}{\sqrt{1/N_{n,a^{\star}}+1/N_{n,a}}}>\frac{\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a^{\star}},a^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b},b}}{\sqrt{1/N_{n,a^{\star}}+1/N_{n,b}}}\quad\text{hence}\quad C_{n}\neq a\>.

This concludes the proof of the first result.

For the
\mathsf{AdaP\text{-}TT}^{\star}
algorithm, the proof is done similarly.
As above, we can construct
\tilde{N}_{2}
with
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[\tilde{N}_{2}]<+\infty
such that, for all
n\geq\tilde{N}_{2}
, we have
B_{n}=a^{\star}
and

\displaystyle\frac{(\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a^{\star}},a^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a},a})\min\{\epsilon/2,\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a^{\star}},a^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a},a}\}}{(\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a^{\star}},a^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b},b})\min\{\epsilon/2,\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a^{\star}},a^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,b},b}\}}\frac{1+N_{n,a^{\star}}/N_{n,b}}{1+N_{n,a^{\star}}/N_{n,a}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{(\Delta_{a}-\eta)\min\{\epsilon/2,\Delta_{a}-\eta\}}{(\Delta_{b}+\eta)\min\{\epsilon/2,\Delta_{b}+\eta\}}\frac{1+(\beta-\tilde{\gamma})/\omega^{\star}_{\epsilon,\beta,b}}{1+(\beta+\tilde{\gamma})/(\omega^{\star}_{\epsilon,\beta,a}+\gamma)}>1\>,

where the last inequality is obtained by taking
\eta
and
\tilde{\gamma}
sufficiently small and by using (
23
)

\frac{\Delta_{a}\min\{\epsilon/2,\Delta_{a}\}}{\Delta_{b}\min\{\epsilon/2,\Delta_{b}\}}\sqrt{\frac{1+\beta/\omega^{\star}_{\epsilon,\beta,b}}{1+\beta/\omega^{\star}_{\epsilon,\beta,a}}}=1\>.

Then, we conclude similarly by using the definition of the TC challenger.
∎

Lemma
50
shows that that the pulling proportion of the best arm converges towards
\beta
for large enough
n
.

Lemma 50
.

Let
\gamma>0
and
T_{\bm{\mu},\gamma}(w)
as in Eq. (
24
).
Then, we have
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[T_{\bm{\mu},\gamma}(\omega^{\star}_{\beta})]<+\infty
(
\mathsf{AdaP\text{-}TT}
) and
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[T_{\bm{\mu},\gamma}(\omega^{\star}_{\epsilon,\beta})]<+\infty
(
\mathsf{AdaP\text{-}TT}^{\star}
).

Proof.

Let
\gamma>0
and
\tilde{\gamma}>0
.
Let
N_{2}
as in Lemma
49
for
\tilde{\gamma}
.
Let
M\geq N_{2}
.
Using Lemmas
47
,
48
and
49
for all
n\geq M
, we obtain that
B_{n}=a^{\star}
,
\left|\frac{N_{n,a^{\star}}}{n-1}-\beta\right|\leq\tilde{\gamma}
and

\exists a\neq a^{\star},\quad\frac{N_{n,a}}{n-1}\geq\omega^{\star}_{\beta,a}+\tilde{\gamma}\quad\implies\quad C_{n}\neq a\>.

For all
a\neq a^{\star}
, let us define
t_{n,a}(\tilde{\gamma})=\max\left\{t\mid M\leq t\leq n,\>N_{t,a}/(n-1)<\omega^{\star}_{\beta,a}+\tilde{\gamma}\right\}
.
Since
N_{t,a}/(n-1)\leq N_{t,a}/(t-1)
for
t\leq n
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{N_{n,a}}{n-1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M-1}{n-1}+\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{t=M}^{n}\mathds{1}\left(a_{t}=C_{t}=a\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M-1}{n-1}+\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{t=M}^{n}\mathds{1}\left(\frac{N_{t,a}}{n-1}<\omega^{\star}_{\beta,a}+\tilde{\gamma},\>a_{t}=C_{t}=a\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M-1}{n-1}+\frac{N_{t_{n,a}(\tilde{\gamma}),a}}{n-1}<\frac{M-1}{n-1}+\omega^{\star}_{\beta,a}+\tilde{\gamma}\>.

The second inequality uses Lemma
49
, and the two last inequalities use the definition of
t_{n,a}(\tilde{\gamma})
.
Using that
\sum_{a\in[K]}\frac{N_{n,a}}{n-1}=\sum_{a\in[K]}\omega^{\star}_{\beta,a}=1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{N_{n,a}}{n-1}

\displaystyle=1-\sum_{b\neq a}\frac{N_{n,a}}{n-1}\geq 1-\sum_{b\neq a}\left(\omega^{\star}_{\beta,b}+\tilde{\gamma}+\frac{M-1}{n-1}\right)=\omega^{\star}_{\beta,a}-(K-1)\left(\tilde{\gamma}+\frac{M-1}{n-1}\right)\>.

Taking
\tilde{\gamma}\leq\gamma/(2(K-1))
and
n\geq\max\{M,2(K-1)(M-1)/\gamma+1\}
yields that

\left\|\frac{N_{n}}{n-1}-\omega^{\star}_{\beta}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\gamma\>.

Let
T_{\bm{\mu},\gamma}(w)
as in Eq. (
24
). Then, we showed that
T_{\bm{\mu},\gamma}(\omega^{\star}_{\beta})\leq\max\{M,2(K-1)(M-1)/\gamma+1\}
.
Therefore, we have

\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[T_{\bm{\mu},\gamma}(\omega^{\star}_{\beta})]\leq\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[\max\{M,2(K-1)(M-1)/\gamma+1\}]<+\infty\>,

which concludes the proof of the first result.

For the
\mathsf{AdaP\text{-}TT}^{\star}
algorithm, the proof is exactly the same by replacing
\omega^{\star}_{\beta}
by
\omega^{\star}_{\epsilon,\beta}
.
∎

G.4
Cost of Doubling and Forgetting

Compared to the generic analysis of Top Two algorithms
Jourdan et al. (2022)
, we need to control the sample complexity cost of the
DAF
(\epsilon)
update (Algorithm
5
).
Due to this reason, we have to pay a multiplicative four-factor: one two-factor due to doubling, and another two-factor due to forgetting.
It is possible to show that this cost exists when adapting any BAI algorithm in which the empirical proportions are converging towards an allocation
\omega
such that
\min_{a}\omega_{a}>0
, i.e. there exists
\omega
such that
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[T_{\bm{\mu},\gamma}(\omega)]<+\infty
.
As shown in Lemma
50
, this is the case for the
\mathsf{AdaP\text{-}TT}
and
\mathsf{AdaP\text{-}TT}^{\star}
algorithms.

Lemma
51
shows that the phase switches of the arms happen in a round-robin fashion, which means that an arm switches phase for a second time after all other arms first switch their own phases.

Lemma 51
.

Let
\omega\in\Sigma_{K}
such that
\min_{a}\omega_{a}>0
.
Assume that there exists
\gamma_{\bm{\mu}}>0
such that for
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[T_{\bm{\mu},\gamma}(\omega)]<+\infty
for all
\gamma\in(0,\gamma_{\bm{\mu}})
, where
T_{\bm{\mu},\gamma}(\omega)
is defined in Equation (
24
).
Let
\eta>0
.
There exists
\tilde{\gamma}_{\bm{\mu}}\in(0,\gamma_{\bm{\mu}})
such that, for all
\gamma\in(0,\tilde{\gamma}_{\bm{\mu}})
, there exists
N_{3}\geq T_{\bm{\mu},\gamma}(\omega)
with
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[N_{3}]<+\infty
which satisfies

\forall n\geq N_{3},\quad\frac{\max_{a\in[K]}T_{k_{n,a}}(a)-1}{\min_{a\in[K]}T_{k_{n,a}}(a)-1}\leq 2+\eta\>.

Proof.

Let
\eta>0
.
Let
\tilde{\gamma}_{\bm{\mu}}\in(0,\gamma_{\bm{\mu}})
such that
2\max_{a\in[K]}(\omega_{a}+\gamma)/(\omega_{a}-\gamma)\leq 2+\eta
, which is possible since
\min_{a}\omega_{a}>0
.
Let
\gamma\in(0,\tilde{\gamma}_{\bm{\mu}})
.
By assumption, we have
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[T_{\bm{\mu},\gamma}(\omega)]<+\infty
.
Then, for all
n\geq T_{\bm{\mu},\gamma}(\omega)
,

\left\|\frac{N_{n}}{n-1}-\omega\right\|_{\infty}\leq\gamma\>.

Let
M\geq T_{\bm{\mu},\gamma}(\omega)
.
Let use denote by
k_{M}=(k_{M,a})_{a\in[K]}
the current phases for all arms
a\in[K]
at time
M
.
Then, for all
n\geq M
and all
a\in[K]
, we have
N_{n,a}\geq(n-1)(\omega_{a}-\gamma)
.
Therefore, taking
n\geq\max_{a\in[K]}2^{k_{M,a}}(\omega_{a}-\gamma)^{-1}+1
, we obtain that
N_{n,a}\geq 2^{k_{M,a}}
for all
a\in[K]
, hence we have
\max_{a\in[K]}T_{k_{M,a}+1}(a)\leq n
.
Since
\min_{a\in[K]}T_{k_{M,a}+1}(a)\geq M
, we have

\max_{a\in[K]}\left|\frac{N_{T_{k_{M,a}+1}(a),a}}{n-1}-\omega_{a}\right|\leq\gamma\>.

Likewise, taking
n\geq\max_{a\in[K]}2^{k_{M,a}+1}(\omega_{a}-\gamma)^{-1}+1
, we obtain that
N_{n,a}\geq 2^{k_{M,a}+1}
for all
a\in[K]
, hence we have
\max_{a\in[K]}T_{k_{M,a}+2}(a)\leq n
.
Let
a_{1}=\argmin_{a\in[K]}T_{k_{M,a}+2}(a)
.
By definition and using Lemma
37
, we have

\displaystyle 2^{k_{M,a_{1}}+1}=N_{T_{k_{M,a_{1}}+2}(a_{1}),a_{1}}\leq(T_{k_{M,a_{1}}+2}(a_{1})-1)(\omega_{a_{1}}+\gamma)\>,

\displaystyle\forall a\neq a_{1},\quad

\displaystyle 2^{k_{M,a}}\leq N_{T_{k_{M,a_{1}}+2}(a_{1}),a}\leq(T_{k_{M,a_{1}}+2}(a_{1})-1)(\omega_{a}+\gamma)\>.

Let
a_{2}=\argmax_{a\in[K]}T_{k_{M,a}+2}(a)
.
By definition and using Lemma
37
, we have

\displaystyle 2^{k_{M,a_{2}}+1}=N_{T_{k_{M,a_{2}}+2}(a_{2}),a_{2}}\geq(T_{k_{M,a_{2}}+2}(a_{2})-1)(\omega_{a_{2}}-\gamma)\>,

Therefore, combining the above yields

\displaystyle(T_{k_{M,a_{2}}+2}(a_{2})-1)\leq(T_{k_{M,a_{1}}+2}(a_{1})-1)2\frac{\omega_{a_{2}}+\gamma}{\omega_{a_{2}}-\gamma}\leq(T_{k_{M,a_{2}}+2}(a_{2})-1)(2+\eta)\>,

where the last inequality uses that
\gamma\in(0,\tilde{\gamma}_{\bm{\mu}})
and
\tilde{\gamma}_{\bm{\mu}}\in(0,\gamma_{\bm{\mu}})
is such that
2\max_{a\in[K]}(\omega_{a}+\gamma)/(\omega_{a}-\gamma)\leq 2+\eta
.
We take
n\geq N_{3}=\max_{a\in[K]}T_{k_{M,a}+2}(a)
, hence we have
k_{n,a}\geq k_{M,a}+2
for all
a\in[K]
.
Since
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[T_{\bm{\mu},\gamma}(\omega)]<+\infty
(i.e. arms are sampled linearly), it is direct to see that
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[\max_{a\in[K]}T_{k_{M,a}+2}(a)]<+\infty
.
This concludes the proof.
∎

G.5
Asymptotic Upper Bound on the Expected Sample Complexity

The final step of the generic analysis of Top Two algorithms
(
Jourdan et al., 2022
)
is to invert the private GLR stopping rule by leveraging the convergence of the empirical proportions towards the
\beta
-optimal allocation.
Provided this convergence is shown, the asymptotic upper bound on the expected sample complexity only depends on the dependence in
\log(1/\delta)
of the threshold that ensures
\delta
-correctness.
Compared to the non-private GLR stopping rule, the private GLR stopping rules pay an extra cost to ensure privacy.
In Section
4.3
, the stopping threshold is adapted with an additive term in
\mathcal{O}(\log(1/\delta)^{2})
.
In Section
4.4
, both the stopping threshold and the transportation costs are modified.

Lemma 52
.

Let
(\delta,\beta)\in(0,1)^{2}
.
Assume that there exists
\gamma_{\bm{\mu}}>0
such that
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[T_{\bm{\mu},\gamma}(\omega^{\star}_{\beta})]<+\infty
for all
\gamma\in(0,\gamma_{\bm{\mu}})
, where
T_{\bm{\mu},\gamma}(w)
is defined in Eq. (
24
).
Combining such a sampling rule, using the
DAF
(\epsilon)
update, with the GLR stopping rule with
W^{G}_{a,b}
as in Eq. (
3
) and the stopping threshold
c^{G,\epsilon}_{a,b}
as in Eq. (
12
) yields a
\delta
-correct algorithm which satisfies that, for all
\bm{\nu}
with mean
\bm{\mu}
such that
|a^{\star}(\bm{\mu})|=1
,

\limsup_{\delta\to 0}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}\left[\tau_{\delta}\right]}{\log(1/\delta)}\leq 4T^{\star}_{\mathrm{KL},\beta}(\bm{\nu})\left(1+\sqrt{1+\frac{\Delta_{\max}^{2}}{2\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{4}}}\right)\>.

where
T^{\star}_{\mathrm{KL},\beta}(\bm{\nu})
as in Eq. (
5
) with
\sigma=1/2
.

Assume that there exists
\gamma_{\bm{\mu}}>0
such that
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[T_{\bm{\mu},\gamma}(\omega^{\star}_{\epsilon,\beta})]<+\infty
.
Combining such a sampling rule, using the
DAF
(\epsilon)
update, with the GLR stopping rule with
W^{G,\epsilon}_{a,b}
as in Eq. (
13
) and the stopping threshold
\tilde{c}^{G,\epsilon}_{a,b}
as in Eq. (
14
) yields a
\delta
-correct algorithm which satisfies that, for all
\bm{\nu}
with mean
\bm{\mu}
such that
|a^{\star}(\bm{\mu})|=1
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{\delta\to 0}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}\left[\tau_{\delta}\right]}{\log(1/\delta)}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}4T^{\star}_{\mathrm{KL},\beta}(\bm{\nu})g_{1}\left(\Delta_{\max}/(\sigma^{2}\epsilon)\right)&\text{if }\Delta_{\max}<3\epsilon\\
12T^{\star}_{\mathrm{KL},\beta}(\bm{\nu}_{G,\epsilon})g_{2}(3\epsilon^{2}T^{\star}_{\mathrm{KL},\beta}(\bm{\nu}_{G,\epsilon})\max\{\beta,1-\beta\}/2)/\sigma^{2}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\>,

where
T^{\star}_{\mathrm{KL},\beta}(\bm{\nu}_{G,\epsilon})
as in Eq. (
15
).
The function
g_{1}(y)=\sup\left\{x\mid x^{2}<x+y\sqrt{2x}+\frac{y^{2}}{4}\right\}
is increasing on
[0,12]
and satisfies that
g_{1}(0)=1
and
g_{1}(12)\leq 10
.
The function
g_{2}(y)=1+2(\sqrt{1+1/y}-1)^{-1}
is increasing on
\mathbb{R}^{\star}_{+}
and satisfies that
\lim_{y\to 0}g_{2}(y)=1
.

Proof.

Lemma
21
and Lemma
23
yields the
\delta
-correctness of both algorithms.

\mathsf{AdaP\text{-}TT}
algorithm.

Let
\zeta>0
,
a^{\star}
be the unique best arm.
Using (
23
) and the continuity of

(\bm{\mu},w)\mapsto\min_{a\neq a^{\star}(\bm{\mu})}\frac{(\mu_{a^{\star}(\bm{\mu})}-\mu_{a})^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}(1/w_{a^{\star}(\bm{\mu})}+1/w_{a})}

yields that there exists
\gamma_{\zeta}>0
such that
\left\|\frac{N_{n}}{n-1}-\omega^{\star}_{\beta}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\gamma_{\zeta}
and
\max_{a\in[K]}|\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a}+1,a}-\mu_{a}|\leq\gamma_{\zeta}
implies that

\displaystyle\forall a\neq a^{\star},\quad

\displaystyle\frac{(\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a^{\star}}+1,a^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a}+1,a})^{2}}{(n-1)/N_{n,a^{\star}}+(n-1)/N_{n,a}}\geq\frac{2\sigma^{2}(1-\zeta)}{T^{\star}_{\mathrm{KL},\beta}(\bm{\nu})}\>,

\displaystyle\frac{n-1}{N_{n,a^{\star}}}+\frac{n-1}{N_{n,a}}\leq\frac{\Delta_{a}^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}(1+\zeta)T^{\star}_{\mathrm{KL},\beta}(\bm{\nu})\>.

We choose such a
\gamma_{\zeta}
.
Let
\gamma_{\bm{\mu}}>0
be such that for
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[T_{\bm{\mu},\gamma}(\omega^{\star}_{\beta})]<+\infty
for all
\gamma\in(0,\gamma_{\bm{\mu}})
, where
T_{\bm{\mu},\gamma}(\omega)
is defined in Eq. (
24
).
Let
\eta>0
.
Let
\tilde{\gamma}_{\bm{\mu}}\in(0,\gamma_{\bm{\mu}})
as in Lemma
51
for this
\eta
.
In the following, let us consider
\gamma\in(0,\min\{\tilde{\gamma}_{\bm{\mu}},\gamma_{\zeta},\beta/4,\Delta_{\min}/4\})
.

Let
N_{3}\geq T_{\bm{\mu},\gamma}(\omega^{\star}_{\beta})
with
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[N_{3}]<+\infty
as Lemma
51
for those
(\gamma,\eta)
.
Then, we have
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[T_{\bm{\mu},\gamma}(\omega^{\star}_{\beta})]<+\infty
and

\forall n\geq N_{3},\quad\frac{\max_{a\in[K]}T_{k_{n,a}}(a)-1}{\min_{a\in[K]}T_{k_{n,a}}(a)-1}\leq 2+\eta\>.

Since arms are sampled linearly, it is direct to construct
N_{4}\geq N_{3}
with
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[N_{4}]<+\infty
such that, for all
n\geq N_{4}
, we have
\max_{a\in[K]}\max_{k\in\{k_{n,a},k_{n,a}+1\}}|\tilde{\mu}_{k,a}-\mu_{a}|\leq\gamma
,
Therefore, we have
\hat{a}_{n}=a^{\star}
.

Let
\kappa\in(0,1)
.
Let
n\geq N_{4}/\kappa
and
(k_{n,a})_{a\in[K]}
be the current phases at time
n
.
Combining the above, we have
\hat{a}_{n}=a^{\star}
and

\displaystyle\max_{a\in[K]}|\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a}+1,a}-\mu_{a}|\leq\gamma\quad,\quad\left\|\frac{N_{n}}{n-1}-\omega^{\star}_{\beta}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\gamma\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{\max_{a\in[K]}T_{k_{n,a}}(a)-1}{\min_{a\in[K]}T_{k_{n,a}}(a)-1}\leq 2+\eta\>.

Let
a_{1}=\argmin_{a\in[K]}T_{k_{n,a}}(a)
and
a_{2}=\argmax_{a\in[K]}T_{k_{n,a}}(a)
.
Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\forall a\neq a^{\star},\quad\frac{(\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,\hat{a}_{n}}+1,\hat{a}_{n}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a}+1,a})^{2}}{1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,\hat{a}_{n}}+1,\hat{a}_{n}}+1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a}+1,a}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a^{\star}}+1,a^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a}+1,a})^{2}}{1/N_{T_{k_{n,a^{\star}}}(a^{\star}),a^{\star}}+1/N_{T_{k_{n,a}}(a),a}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{(\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a^{\star}}+1,a^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a}+1,a})^{2}}{1/N_{T_{k_{n,a_{1}}}(a_{1}),a^{\star}}+1/N_{T_{k_{n,a_{1}}}(a_{1}),a}}

\displaystyle\geq(\min_{a\in[K]}T_{k_{n,a}}(a)-1)\frac{2\sigma^{2}(1-\zeta)}{T^{\star}_{\mathrm{KL},\beta}(\bm{\nu})}\>.

Similarly, we can show that, for all
a\neq a^{\star}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a^{\star}}+1,a^{\star}}}+\frac{1}{\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a}+1,a}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N_{T_{k_{n,a^{\star}}}(a^{\star}),a^{\star}}}+\frac{1}{N_{T_{k_{n,a}}(a),a}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N_{T_{k_{n,a_{1}}}(a_{1}),a^{\star}}}+\frac{1}{N_{T_{k_{n,a_{1}}}(a_{1}),a}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\min_{a\in[K]}T_{k_{n,a}}(a)-1}\frac{\Delta_{a}^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}(1+\zeta)T^{\star}_{\mathrm{KL},\beta}(\bm{\nu})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\min_{a\in[K]}T_{k_{n,a}}(a)-1}\frac{\Delta_{\max}^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}(1+\zeta)T^{\star}_{\mathrm{KL},\beta}(\bm{\nu})\>.

Let
c^{G}_{a,b}
as in Eq. (
4
).
Using Lemma
37
, we obtain, for all
a\neq a^{\star}
,

\displaystyle c^{G}_{a^{\star},a}(\tilde{N}_{k_{n}+1},\delta(2\zeta(s)^{2}(k_{n,a^{\star}}+1)^{s}(k_{n,a}+1)^{s})^{-1})\leq 4\log(4+(\max_{b\in[K]}k_{n,b}-1)\log 2)

\displaystyle\quad+2\mathcal{C}_{G}\left(\log(1/\delta)/2+s\log(\max_{b\in[K]}k_{n,b}-1)+\log(2(K-1)\zeta(s)^{2})/2\right)

Likewise, we obtain, for all
a\in[K]
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}}\sum_{c\in\{a^{\star},a\}}\frac{1}{\tilde{N}_{k_{n,c},c}}\left(\log\frac{2K\zeta(s)(k_{n,c}+1)^{s}}{\delta}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\Delta_{\max}^{2}}{2\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{4}}\frac{(1+\zeta)T^{\star}_{\mathrm{KL},\beta}(\bm{\nu})}{\min_{b\in[K]}T_{k_{n,b}}(b)-1}\left(\log(1/\delta)+s\log(\max_{b\in[K]}k_{n,b}+1)+\log(2K\zeta(s))\right)^{2}

Let us denote by
T^{+}_{k_{n}+1}=\max_{b\in[K]}T_{k_{n,b}+1}(b)
,
T^{+}_{k_{n}+2}=\max_{b\in[K]}T_{k_{n,b}+2}(b)
,
T^{-}_{k_{n}+1}=\min_{b\in[K]}T_{k_{n,b}+1}(b)
,
T^{-}_{k_{n}}=\min_{b\in[K]}T_{k_{n,b}}(b)
.
Let
T
be a time such that
T\geq T^{+}_{k_{n}+1}\geq\kappa T
.
Using Lemmas
37
and
51
, we have

\displaystyle(k_{n,b}-1)\log 2=\log N_{T_{k_{n,b}}(b),b}\leq\log T_{k_{n,b}}(b)\leq\log T^{+}_{k_{n}}\leq\log T^{-}_{k_{n}}+\log(2+\eta)\>.

Using the
DAF
(\epsilon)
update with the GLR stopping rule with
W^{G}_{a,b}
as in Eq. (
3
) and the stopping threshold
c^{G,\epsilon}_{a,b}
as in Eq. (
12
), we have

\displaystyle\min\left\{\tau_{\delta},T\right\}-\kappa T\leq\sum_{T\geq T^{+}_{k_{n}}\geq\kappa T}(T^{+}_{k_{n}+2}-T^{+}_{k_{n}+1})\mathds{1}\left(\tau_{\delta}>T^{+}_{k_{n}+1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{T^{+}_{k_{n}}=\kappa T}^{T}(T^{+}_{k_{n}+2}-T^{+}_{k_{n}+1})\mathds{1}\left(\exists a\neq a^{\star},\frac{(\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a^{\star}}+1,a^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a}+1,a})^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a^{\star}}+1,a^{\star}}}+\frac{1}{\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a}+1,a}}\right)}<c^{G,\epsilon}_{a^{\star},a}(\tilde{N}_{k_{n}+1},\delta)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{T\geq T^{+}_{k_{n}}\geq\kappa T}(T^{+}_{k_{n}+2}-T^{+}_{k_{n}+1})\mathds{1}\left((T^{-}_{k_{n}}-1)\frac{1-\zeta}{T^{\star}_{\mathrm{KL},\beta}(\bm{\nu})}<8\log(4+\log T^{-}_{k_{n}}+\log(2+\eta))\right.

\displaystyle+4\mathcal{C}_{G}\left(\log(1/\delta)/2+s\log(2+\log_{2}T^{-}_{k_{n}}+\log_{2}(2+\eta))+\log(2(K-1)\zeta(s)^{2})/2\right)

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{\Delta_{\max}^{2}}{2\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{4}}\frac{(1+\zeta)T^{\star}_{\mathrm{KL},\beta}(\bm{\nu})}{T^{-}_{k_{n}}-1}\left(\log(1/\delta)+s\log(2+\log_{2}T^{-}_{k_{n}}+\log_{2}(2+\eta))+\log(2K\zeta(s))\right)^{2}\right)\>,

Let
T_{\zeta}(\delta)
defined as the largest deterministic time such that the above condition is satisfied when replacing
T^{-}_{k_{n}}
by
(1-\kappa)T
.
Let
k_{\delta}
be the largest random vector of phases such that that
T^{+}_{k_{\delta}+1}\leq T_{\zeta}(\delta)
almost surely, hence
T^{+}_{k_{\delta}+2}>T_{\zeta}(\delta)
almost surely.
Then, using the above yields that
\tau_{\delta}\leq T^{+}_{k_{\delta}+2}
almost surely, hence

\limsup_{\delta\to 0}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[\tau_{\delta}]}{\log(1/\delta)}\leq\limsup_{\delta\to 0}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[T^{+}_{k_{\delta}+2}]}{\log(1/\delta)}\leq(2+\eta)^{2}\limsup_{\delta\to 0}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[T^{+}_{k_{\delta}+1}]}{\log(1/\delta)}\leq(2+\eta)^{2}\limsup_{\delta\to 0}\frac{T_{\zeta}(\delta)}{\log(1/\delta)}

where the second inequality uses Lemma
51
twice, i.e.
T^{+}_{k_{\delta}+2}\leq(2+\eta)T^{-}_{k_{\delta}+2}\leq(2+\eta)^{2}T^{+}_{k_{\delta}+1}
, and the last one used the definition of
k_{\delta}
and that
T_{\zeta}(\delta)
is deterministic.

Since we are only interested in upper bounding
\limsup_{\delta\to 0}\frac{T_{\zeta}(\delta)}{\log(1/\delta)}
, we can safely drop the second orders terms in
T
and
\log(1/\delta)
.
This allows us to remove the terms in
\mathcal{O}(\log\log T)
and in
\mathcal{O}(\log\log(1/\delta))
.
Using that
\mathcal{C}_{G}(x)=x+\mathcal{O}(\log x)
, tedious manipulations yields that

\limsup_{\delta\to 0}\frac{T_{\zeta}(\delta)}{\log(1/\delta)}\leq\frac{T^{\star}_{\mathrm{KL},\beta}(\bm{\nu})}{1-\kappa}D_{\zeta}(\mu,\epsilon)\>,

where

\displaystyle D_{\zeta}(\mu,\epsilon)=\sup\left\{x\mid x^{2}<\frac{2}{1-\zeta}x+\frac{1+\zeta}{1-\zeta}\frac{\Delta_{\max}^{2}}{2\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{4}}\right\}\leq\frac{1}{1-\zeta}\left(1+\sqrt{1+(1-\zeta^{2})\frac{\Delta_{\max}^{2}}{2\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{4}}}\right)\>.

The last inequality uses that
x^{2}-2bx-c<0
for all
x\in[0,b(1+\sqrt{1+c/b^{2}}))
.
Therefore, we have shown that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\delta\to 0}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[\tau_{\delta}]}{\log(1/\delta)}\leq(2+\eta)^{2}\frac{T^{\star}_{\mathrm{KL},\beta}(\bm{\nu})}{(1-\kappa)(1-\zeta)}\left(1+\sqrt{1+(1-\zeta^{2})\frac{\Delta_{\max}^{2}}{2\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{4}}}\right)\>.

Letting
\kappa
,
\eta
and
\zeta
goes to zero concludes the proof of the first result.

\mathsf{AdaP\text{-}TT}^{\star}
algorithm.

For the
\mathsf{AdaP\text{-}TT}^{\star}
algorithm, the proof is done with similar arguments.
Using (
23
) and the continuity of
(\bm{\mu},w)\to\min_{a\neq a^{\star}}W^{G,\epsilon}_{a^{\star},a}(\bm{\mu},w)
, defined in Eq. (
13
), we obtain another
\gamma_{\zeta}>0
such that
\left\|\frac{N_{n}}{n-1}-\omega^{\star}_{\epsilon,\beta}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\gamma_{\zeta}
and
\max_{a\in[K]}|\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a}+1,a}-\mu_{a}|\leq\gamma_{\zeta}
implies that

\displaystyle\forall a\neq a^{\star},\quad

\displaystyle\frac{(\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a^{\star}}+1,a^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a}+1,a})\min\{\epsilon/2,\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a^{\star}}+1,a^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a}+1,a}\}}{(n-1)/N_{n,a^{\star}}+(n-1)/N_{n,a}}\geq\frac{2\sigma^{2}(1-\zeta)}{T^{\star}_{\mathrm{KL},\beta}(\bm{\nu}_{G,\epsilon})}\>,

\displaystyle\frac{n-1}{N_{n,a^{\star}}}+\frac{n-1}{N_{n,a}}\leq\frac{\Delta_{a}\min\{\epsilon/2,\Delta_{a}\}}{2\sigma^{2}}(1+\zeta)T^{\star}_{\mathrm{KL},\beta}(\bm{\nu}_{G,\epsilon})\>.

We choose such a
\gamma_{\zeta}
.
Let
\gamma_{\bm{\mu}}>0
be such that for
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\nu}}[T_{\bm{\mu},\gamma}(\omega^{\star}_{\epsilon,\beta})]<+\infty
for all
\gamma\in(0,\gamma_{\bm{\mu}})
.
Let
\eta>0
.
Let
\tilde{\gamma}_{\bm{\mu}}\in(0,\gamma_{\bm{\mu}})
as in Lemma
51
for this
\eta
.
In the following, let us consider
\gamma\in(0,\min\{\tilde{\gamma}_{\mu},\gamma_{\zeta},\beta/4,\Delta_{\min}/4,(\epsilon/2-\max_{a,\Delta_{a}<\epsilon/2}\Delta_{a})/2\})
.

Let
\kappa\in(0,1)
.
As above, we can construct
N_{3}
with Lemma
51
and
N_{4}\geq N_{3}
such that
\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[N_{4}]<+\infty
.
Let
n\geq N_{4}/\kappa
and
(k_{n,a})_{a\in[K]}
the current phases.
Then, we have
\hat{a}_{n}=a^{\star}
,

\displaystyle\max_{a\in[K]}|\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a}+1,a}-\mu_{a}|\leq\gamma\>,\quad\left\|\frac{N_{n}}{n-1}-\omega^{\star}_{\epsilon,\beta}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\gamma\>\text{and}\>\frac{\max_{a\in[K]}T_{k_{n,a}}(a)-1}{\min_{a\in[K]}T_{k_{n,a}}(a)-1}\leq 2+\eta\>.

Depending on the value of the private empirical gap, the stopping condition that is checked is different.
For all
a\neq a^{\star}
such that
\Delta_{a}<\epsilon/2
, we have
\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a^{\star}}+1,a^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a}+1,a}\leq\Delta_{a}+2\gamma<\epsilon/2
.
For all
a\neq a^{\star}
such that
\Delta_{a}\geq\epsilon/2
, we have either
\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a^{\star}}+1,a^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a}+1,a}\geq\epsilon/2
or
\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a^{\star}}+1,a^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a}+1,a}\leq\epsilon/2
and
\Delta_{a}\leq\epsilon/2+2\gamma
.
Let
a_{1}=\argmin_{a\in[K]}T_{k_{n,a}}(a)
and
a_{2}=\argmax_{a\in[K]}T_{k_{n,a}}(a)
.
Therefore, we obtain similarly that, for all
a\neq a^{\star}
,

\displaystyle\frac{(\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a^{\star}}+1,a^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a}+1,a})\min\{\epsilon/2,\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a^{\star}}+1,a^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a}+1,a}\}}{1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a^{\star}}+1,a^{\star}}+1/\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a}+1,a}}\geq(\min_{b\in[K]}T_{k_{n,b}}(b)-1)\frac{2\sigma^{2}(1-\zeta)}{T^{\star}_{\mathrm{KL},\beta}(\bm{\nu}_{G,\epsilon})}\>.

Similarly, for all
a\neq a^{\star}
such that
\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a^{\star}}+1,a^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a}+1,a}\leq\epsilon/2
, hence
\Delta_{a}\leq\epsilon/2+2\gamma
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a^{\star}}+1,a^{\star}}}+\frac{1}{\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a}+1,a}}\leq\frac{1}{\min_{b\in[K]}T_{k_{n,b}}(b)-1}\frac{\Delta_{a}\min\{\epsilon/2,\Delta_{a}\}}{2\sigma^{2}}(1+\zeta)T^{\star}_{\mathrm{KL},\beta}(\bm{\nu}_{G,\epsilon})\>,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a^{\star}}+1,a^{\star}}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\tilde{N}_{k_{n,a}+1,a}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{(\min_{b\in[K]}T_{k_{n,b}}(b)-1)}\frac{\Delta_{a}\min\{\epsilon/2,\Delta_{a}\}}{\sigma^{2}}(1+\zeta)T^{\star}_{\mathrm{KL},\beta}(\bm{\nu}_{G,\epsilon})},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}}\sum_{c\in\{a^{\star},a\}}\frac{1}{\tilde{N}_{k_{n,c}+1,c}}\left(\log\frac{3K(k_{n,c}+1)^{s}\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\Delta_{a}\min\{\epsilon/2,\Delta_{a}\}(1+\zeta)T^{\star}_{\mathrm{KL},\beta}(\bm{\nu}_{G,\epsilon})}{4\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{4}(\min_{b\in[K]}T_{k_{n,b}}(b)-1)}\left(\log(1/\delta)+s\log(\max_{b\in[K]}k_{n,b}+1)+\log(3K\zeta(s))\right)^{2}\>,

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\epsilon\sigma}\sum_{c\in\{a^{\star},a\}}\sqrt{\frac{h(\tilde{N}_{k_{n,c}+1,c},\delta)}{\tilde{N}_{k_{n,c}+1,c}}}\log\left(\frac{3K\zeta(s)(k_{n,c}+1)^{s}}{\delta}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{2\Delta_{a}\min\{\epsilon/2,\Delta_{a}\}(1+\zeta)T^{\star}_{\mathrm{KL},\beta}(\bm{\nu}_{G,\epsilon})}{\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{4}(\min_{b\in[K]}T_{k_{n,b}}(b)-1)}}

\displaystyle\quad\sqrt{h(2^{\max_{b\in[K]}k_{n,b}-1},\delta)}\left(\log(1/\delta)+s\log(\max_{b\in[K]}k_{n,b}+1)+\log(3K\zeta(s))\right)\>.

Moreover, for all
a\neq a^{\star}
such that
\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a^{\star}}+1,a^{\star}}-\tilde{\mu}_{k_{n,a}+1,a}\geq 3\epsilon
, hence
\Delta_{a}\geq 3\epsilon
, we have
